# Orbital Overhaul — STATUS

Last updated: 2026-08-09 · Build version: **CS024 P6d — `startLevel` GATE-TOOLING DEBUG KNOB** (`GAME_VERSION` still **"1.0.0.22"** — CS024 P7 owns the bump, straight to `"1.0.0.24"`, deliberately skipping `.23`). **⛔ GATE B IS OPEN AND BLOCKING — see `## Playtest asks`. P7 MUST NOT RUN until it is answered there.**

**CS024 P6d — ANOTHER IN-ROUND CORRECTIVE PHASE, NOT IN THE ORIGINAL PLAN: GATE TOOLING, NOT A LEVER.** Per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §4.5. Gate B needs to sample a deep level without playing thirty levels to reach it, and nothing in the build could jump the level counter — this phase gives it one knob. **`startLevel`** joins the registry's GLOBAL section (def 1, min 1, max 99, step 1) — a **plain flat knob**, not a lever: no `▼`/`↳` glyph, no `(inv)`, no floor/ceil/steps triple, no `LEVERS` entry. `startGame()` seeds `game.wave = DEBUG.startLevel - 1`, so `nextWave()` lands the first level on exactly the knob's value; everything downstream (`leverState`, `payloadSlots`, `musicIntensity`) was already a pure function of `game.wave`, so nothing else needed to change — this was verified, not assumed (`scratchpad/test-cs024-p6d.js` §B pins `leverState(N)` identical whether reached by seed or by play, at N = 1, 2, 9, 33, 60, 99).

**⛔ THE FIDELITY LIMIT IS REAL AND DELIBERATELY UNFIXED: a run started at level N gives a level-N FIELD with a level-1 SHIP.** No scoop upgrades, no banked powerups, no accumulated garbage, no towed load — it is a spawn-side sampling tool, not a simulation of having played there. Documented at the knob itself (a comment on the registry entry) so a future session doesn't mistake it for more than it is. **Explicitly not "fixed" by seeding scoop level or powerups** — that would have been unrequested design, and the phase prompt said so directly.

**A RUN STARTED ABOVE LEVEL 1 CANNOT RECORD, AND THE MECHANISM IS A STICKY FLAG READ ONCE.** `game.debugRun = DEBUG.startLevel > 1` is set exactly once, inside `startGame()`, from the knob's value at that instant — `DEBUG.startLevel` is never re-read for the rest of the run, so dragging the slider back to 1 mid-run cannot silently re-arm recording for a run that started at 33, and dragging it up mid-run cannot retroactively void a legitimate one (`test-cs024-p6d.js` §F pins both directions). Three **persistence points** are gated on it, in-memory state left alone so the HUD/gameover screen/toasts behave exactly as they would in a real run (§G): (1) the initials-entry screen (`game.entry`) never arms on a debug run regardless of score, so `HighScores.add()` — reachable only through `commitEntry()` — is never called; (2) `Achievements.save()` is the single choke point both the achievement-unlock commit (`onUnlock`) and the periodic lifetime-stats flush (`tick`) already funneled through, so gating it once inside the function covers both named persistence points at their one real call site. **A visible tell** — `"DEBUG RUN"` in `COLOR.dim`, centered top of the HUD, reflows nothing — so a good score silently failing to record is never baffling (`drawHUD()`, gated on `game.debugRun`, ahead of the score readout).

**KNOWN, ACCEPTED CONSEQUENCE OF "GATE THE PERSISTENCE POINTS, NOT THE IN-MEMORY COUNTERS" (the phase prompt's own instruction, not a guess this session made):** `Achievements.lifetime.*` counters (e.g. `maxWave`) DO advance in memory during a debug run, same as a real one — a debug run reaching level 40 really does raise `lifetime.maxWave` to 40 in memory. That write is never flushed to storage *during* the debug run, but the tainted in-memory value persists until the process reloads, so if the very next game (a real one) triggers a save before a reload, the debug run's high-water mark rides along in that legitimate save. `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §4.5 asks for exactly this shape ("gate the three persistence points, not the in-memory counters" — this file's own wording, verbatim, in the phase prompt), so it is not treated as a bug to silently fix; noted here in case Gate B surfaces it as a confusing lifetime-stat jump.

**CS024 P6c — AN IN-ROUND CORRECTIVE PHASE, NOT IN THE ORIGINAL PLAN. EVERY LEVER NOW HAS THREE KNOBS INSTEAD OF ONE, AND GATE B CAN ACTUALLY TUNE A RAMP.** Per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §2.6, rewritten for this phase. **What was wrong:** P5's instruction — "one knob per lever" — was incoherent, because **a lever is not a value.** Its shipped quantity is derived from a floor, a ceiling, a step count and `game.wave`, so the single row P5 shipped could do only one thing when touched: **pin that lever to a flat constant at every level.** It could not move the ramp's floor, its ceiling or its period; and because its `min`/`max` came from `Math.min`/`Math.max` of the shipped pair, it could not move an endpoint **past** its partner either — on the seven INVERTED levers that meant a ceiling could never be raised above where it already sat. Gate B's entire job is tuning ramps.

**(1) 51 ROWS, THREE PER LEVER — `<leverId>Floor`, `<leverId>Ceil`, `<leverId>Steps`.** `leverKnob()` returns them as an ARRAY, spread into `DEBUG_VARS`. Moving any of the three re-derives that lever's **whole ramp immediately, at every level**. **The flat pin is SUBSUMED, not lost:** setting Floor equal to Ceil pins the lever to a constant at every level, which is exactly what the retired row did — there is deliberately no fourth row for it. **Registry 33 → 67** (16 non-lever knobs untouched + 51). Section order and the nine headers are unchanged; P6's POWERUPS rows are untouched.

**(2) `leverState()` IS SPLIT, AND PURITY IS INTACT — THE SPLIT IS WHAT MAKES THE KNOBS POSSIBLE.** The arithmetic moved into **`leverValues(table, wave)`** (same closed form, taking the table it walks as an argument), and `leverState(wave)` is that applied to the shipped `LEVERS`. The panel's knobs go in through **`liveLevers(wave)`**, which is `leverValues` over a DEBUG-overridden copy of the table, and which lives **outside** the sliced odometer section because it reads `DEBUG` and `leverState` must not. **A table parameter rather than P5's `DEBUG.x ?? lv.x` at the consumers, and the reason is structural, not stylistic:** a Steps override changes a driver's **wrap period**, which changes how many carries every dependent under it receives, so it cannot be expressed as a post-hoc override of `leverState`'s OUTPUT — it has to go in before the derivation runs, and the only way in is the table. The bare-context slice test passes **unmodified**; `function leverState(wave) {` is byte-identical as a line, which is what three phases of slice tests find by prefix.

**⛔ `leverState()` NOW HAS NO IN-GAME CALLER, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE — DO NOT DELETE IT ON A CLEANUP PASS.** All twelve consumer call sites read `liveLevers`. `leverState` is what `liveLevers` is *defined against*: the odometer's pure statement of the shipped ramp, the bare-context surface the headless tests evaluate on its own, and the reference the untouched-panel invariant is pinned to (an untouched registry must make `liveLevers(w) === leverState(w)` at every level). It says so at the site.

**(3) THE RANGES SPAN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** Floor and Ceil share one range, widened by a **full span on each side** of the shipped pair (clamped at 0, since every levered quantity is a non-negative count/speed/interval/angle), so either endpoint can be dragged past the other. `coalescePause`'s pair now sliders over [0, 8.5] where P5 gave it [1.5, 5]. **Nothing asserts or clamps `floor <= ceil`, in the panel any more than in the table** — `Math.min`/`Math.max` appear in `leverKnob` only to find the shipped pair's extent for that widening, and the test pins that as the single such line in the build.

**(4) `junkCount` IS ROUNDED AT THE CONSUMER, AND THIS IS NEW.** It is the one integer-valued lever — everything else is a speed, an interval, a multiplier or an angle. At the shipped 3/12/10 every step lands on a whole number, so nothing had ever broken; **the Steps knob breaks it** (3/12/**7** interpolates to 3, 4.5, 6, 7.5, 9, 10.5, 12). `nextWave()` now spawns `Math.round(lv.junkCount)`. **Round, not floor, deliberately:** it is the nearest achievable count to the authored curve (off by at most half a satellite rather than up to a whole one), it returns both authored endpoints exactly when they are whole, it stays monotonic, and flooring would shave every interior step of every retune downward — a systematic bias making mid-ramp levels quietly easier than the number on the slider while the difficulty log lied by a full satellite in the same direction every time. Rounding at `nextWave()` rather than inside `spawnFieldSatellites()` is what keeps the log honest: it is handed the same local.

**(5) THE PANEL SHOWS THE CHAIN, NOT JUST THE ROWS — AND THE MARKERS ARE DERIVED FROM `LEVERS`, NEVER TYPED.** A **driver** wears `▼` and sits unindented ("everything indented below moves when this one wraps"); a **dependent** wears a two-space `↳`; an **inverted** lever wears ` (inv)` on all three of its rows, so a Floor numerically above its Ceil reads as correct rather than as a bug at 2am; a **non-lever knob** wears neither glyph nor indent, because it belongs to no chain. `everyNLevels` decides the first, `floor > ceil` the second — both read off the table inside `leverKnob()`, so a future table edit cannot desync the panel from the mechanism (the test proves the derivation by mutating a lever's `everyNLevels` and flipping a floor/ceil, and watching the labels follow).

**LABEL WIDTH IS A REAL CONSTRAINT AND IT COST THE LEVER LABELS THEIR LONG NAMES.** `drawDebug` neither wraps nor truncates, and the label column is **~32 monospace characters** at 18px before it reaches a selected row's `◄` chevron (documented at `DEBUG_VALUE_X` and asserted in the test). The glyphs, the indent and ` (inv)` cost up to 18 of those on their own, so the BASE labels were shortened rather than the markers dropped: the ten UFO levers dropped their "UFO " prefix (their header already says it), sizes became bare `lg`/`md`/`sm`, and `Coalescence inert delay` became `Coalesce delay`. JUNK and HUNTER kept their prefixes because both own a "speed md"/"speed sm" pair and the prefix is the only thing telling those apart once the header has scrolled off the window. Longest label in the registry: 31 characters.

**(6) THE `def: null` "auto" SENTINEL IS RETIRED, AND SO IS THE DESIGN QUESTION P5 FLAGGED.** A lever's VALUE varies per level and had no honest default; a floor, a ceiling and a step count are plain constants that do, so every one of the 51 rows ships with the shipped table field as its `def`. That makes the untouched-panel guarantee **sharper** than the sentinel did — an untouched registry reproduces `LEVERS` field for field, pinned at every level 1..200 against `leverState` and against HEAD — and it retired both null special-cases the sentinel needed (`menuDebug`'s nudge base, `drawDebug`'s `"auto"` string), which went with it. **P5's open question ("does pinning a lever flat on touch feel right?") no longer needs an answer**: pinning flat is now one specific thing you can choose (Floor = Ceil), not the only thing a slider can do.
