**This session (CS023 P4b, landed):** implemented CS023 Phase 4b per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS023.md` Correction C15 and the re-resolved FORK-CS023-H, re-grepping every P4 anchor by symbol per the phase prompt's own instruction (every anchor in the spec is an estimate that drifts the moment a phase lands). Scope was one deleted gate, three renamed constants, one renamed knob id, and the set of inverted field-level assertions this correction requires in `test-cs023-p4.js` — plus the eleven-file repoint sweep and two determinism-gap fixes that followed from it. See the top summary above for the full write-up; this block is kept short to avoid restating it.

**This session (CS023 P4, landed):** implemented CS023 Phase 4 per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS023.md` §1.5/§4.7/§4.8, Correction C14 and FORK-CS023-B/C/H, re-grepping every anchor by symbol (P1–P3 had all moved things — in particular `debrisBounce`'s P3-renamed `aFixed`/`bFixed` dispatch, and the two new collision passes now sitting between P2's pass and Cleanup). Scope was the seven phase-prompt items, the new test file, and a twelve-file repoint sweep.

**ONE DERIVATION WENT A STEP BEYOND THE LITERAL PHASE PROMPT AND IS FLAGGED HERE RATHER THAN BURIED.** Item 7 says to add two registry rows, `orbitGravityAccel` and `debrisBounceRestitution`. Adding the ROW alone would have shipped a **dead slider**: `debrisBounce` read the constant `DEBRIS_BOUNCE_RESTITUTION` directly, so moving the knob would have changed nothing. Three things in the already-shipped material say the knob is meant to be live. (1) P2's own constants-block comment, written against this same spec, reads *"(CS023 P4 exposes it as a live debug slider; deliberately NOT a knob yet.)"* — "exposes it as a live debug slider" is a functional claim, not a registry-row one. (2) The playtest gate's instructions say the four densities, both orbit velocities, gravity acceleration **and bounce restitution** "are live sliders in the hidden Debug panel's ORBIT section. **Retune in-session and report the number you landed on, not a yes/no**" — a slider that changes nothing cannot be retuned. (3) Gate question 9 names it explicitly: *"(knob: `debrisBounceRestitution`)"*. So `debrisBounce` now reads `DEBUG.debrisBounceRestitution` at its three restitution sites, which is not a new pattern but the one **every** ORBIT knob already follows: the constant supplies the registry `def`, the consumer reads `DEBUG.*` at the site. Shipped behaviour is byte-identical until the slider moves (the knob seeds from the const), and — the detail that confirms the convention rather than merely permitting it — **`test-cs023-p2.js`'s restitution SANDBOX keeps working completely unchanged**, because it patches the CONSTANT, which re-seeds the `def`, which re-seeds the knob. This was recorded as a derivation rather than surfaced as a blocking design question because it invents no number and no rule; if Paul disagrees, backing it out is a two-token revert at three sites plus this note.

**A FINDING THE TESTS SURFACED, WHICH IS NOT A BUG AND WAS NOT INVENTED AROUND.** The arming rule has two distance tests: a GLOBAL one ("nothing live inside `ORBIT_GRAVITY_TRIGGER_R`") and a PER-BODY one ("this body is beyond `ORBIT_GRAVITY_TARGET_R`"). Because the target radius (676) sits INSIDE the trigger radius (814), **any body in the 676–814 band blocks the whole pass — including itself**. It follows that whenever arming runs at all, every live debris body is already at ≥ 814, and the per-body target test can never exclude anything: it is unreachable, in this build, by construction. It was implemented exactly as §4.7 specifies anyway, because it is correct belt-and-braces — it is what keeps the rule self-consistent if a future geometry retune ever put the target OUTSIDE the trigger — but **the observable arming rule in the shipped build is the trigger alone**, and the test file says so in its own section rather than asserting a boundary that cannot be reached. Two consequences worth carrying into P5's doc sweep: the GDD should describe the band, not just the two radii; and the first test staging that was written (a body at 700 px expected to arm) was WRONG about the mechanic, which is how the finding turned up.

**The cap's ring scan got its own test section, because it is the assertion a plausible-looking mutant fails.** `maxOrbitSpeed()` is checked against an independently recomputed `max` over rings of `angVel × radius` at the shipped `[2, 4]` (255.7 px/s, argmax ring 4) and then, in a **sandbox build whose `ORBIT_FAST_RING` literal is patched to `[1, 2]`**, against ring 2's **169.0** rather than the outermost ring's **85.2** — the C14 case, as a live control. Lists of length 0, 1, 2, 3 and 4 all scan correctly (an EMPTY list is the one case where the outermost ring genuinely IS the fastest, which is exactly why the shortcut survives casual testing). Doubling `DEBUG.orbitAngVel` doubles the cap; doubling `DEBUG.orbitFastMult` doubles it; dropping `DEBUG.orbitCount` to 3 moves the argmax to ring 2 and lowers it; `orbitAngVel` at 0 returns a clean 0, not a NaN. The cap is unchanged by `startGame()`, by `nextWave()` and by the ramp, because it describes the ARCHETYPE and not tonight's rings. **Both radii are proven derived twice over** — a source pin that neither declaration line carries the literal 814 or 676, and a behavioural one that patches `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` to 200 in a sandbox and shows the trigger become 1,000 and the target 800 (and a second sandbox moving `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS` to 500, giving 914/776).

**"Once per frame, not once per body" is proven with an instrumented copy of the real source**, the same method CS022 P3 and CS023 P2 used for their frame-budget gates: a counter injected into `maxOrbitSpeed`'s own body, then 30 real `update(1/60)` frames with **40** simultaneously-drifting bodies. Result: **exactly 30 calls**, against the 1,200 a per-body call would have produced.

**The exact per-frame arithmetic is asserted to 1e-9 over a fall long enough to reach the cap.** A size-3 body staged 1,900 px from the dock on a pure radial line: the inward component grows by exactly `DEBUG.orbitGravityAccel × dt` for **511 consecutive frames**, then **plateaus at exactly `maxOrbitSpeed()`** for the rest of the fall and never once exceeds it, and the ramp's duration matches `cap / accel` (8.52 s) to within a frame. The release keeps the accumulated speed to the float (no damping), that speed is the cap itself, and the piece then **coasts on** more than 200 px further inside the shell region rather than parking. The tangential proof runs separately: a body given 300 px/s of pure tangential drift has its perpendicular component **exactly** preserved by the force on every single frame, while its total speed legitimately rises **above** the cap — and at the peak, total is exactly `hypot(cap, that frame's tangential component)`. (The tangential MAGNITUDE does evolve frame to frame, because a flat central force conserves `r × v`; the first draft of that assertion wrongly expected a constant 300 px/s and had to be corrected to the real claim, which is that the FORCE never touches it.)

**All four of Paul's interrupts are driven for real, not asserted from the source.** Arrival (through 600 real frames); satellite contact and UFO contact (through the actual debris-vs-debris and UFO-vs-debris passes, with the rail-borne partner's rail invariant intact and its `orbitRadius`/`orbitAngVel`/`size`/`radius`/`dead` byte-unchanged); a player shot (a real `Bullet` through `update()`'s bullet pass); and a ship ram (through the real hazards-vs-ship block, hull down, score unmoved). **FLAG-CS023-c is verified rather than assumed**, and isolating it took care: the three children are born coincident, so the debris-vs-debris pass later in the SAME frame bounces them and stamps `drifting = false` — which is *not* inheritance. The claim is therefore proved two ways: directly after a `destroyDebris` call with no frame in between (`drifting` genuinely ABSENT, `"drifting" in child === false`), and behaviourally with a blocker parked inside the trigger radius so the arming pass cannot run at all, over 90 frames.

**Edge cases, all through real code paths.** `game.dock` is re-created by every `nextWave()`: an armed piece carried from level 3 to level 6 keeps its force across the boundary (FLAG-CS023-b, sticky — the fresh shell blocks arming, which is the point) and the very next frame's acceleration lands along the vector to the **new** dock, with the old dock's direction as a live control that must NOT match. A real `resizeWorld` shrink (3840×2160 → 2560×1440) leaves `drifting` intact and re-homes the armed piece **identically to an unarmed control** — same bearing, same `dmax` clamp — because `resizeWorld` knows nothing about the drift. The realistic route (orbit level → field level through `nextWave()`) leaves the flag set and the pass inert, with no velocity moving by a single float over 120 frames. A destroyed armed piece leaves an empty array and normal canisters, none of which carries the field. And the seam case is built the way the torus actually bites: a dock at x = 30 and a body 900 px away **the short way round, across the wrap**, where naive subtraction says −2,940 px — the force pushes **+x**, by exactly one frame of acceleration, with zero spurious y, and the piece genuinely arrives across the seam.

**Mutation-tested against seventeen deliberate breakages, every one caught, and most of them behaviourally rather than by a source pin:** the C14 outer-ring shortcut (7 failures); the force moved inside the `blocked` gate, i.e. non-sticky arming (305); capping total speed instead of the radial component (645); arming rail-borne bodies (3); a release that parks the body (3); `drifting` initialised in the constructor (12); a radius written as the literal 814 (4); the disarm line deleted from `debrisBounce` (9); the knobs inserted rather than appended (4); naive non-wrap arithmetic for the dock vector (480); the cap read per body (1,401); an inclusive trigger comparison so ring 4 blocks itself (2); the pass moved after the collision passes (750); the FORK-CS023-H gate dropped (3); the force reading the const instead of the knob, killing the A/B (3); the target radius used as the trigger (4); and the restitution left on the const, i.e. a dead knob (2).

**The repoint sweep was four times what the spec predicted, and the lesson is the standing one: grep, don't trust the list.** FLAG-CS023-o says "two test files pin the count." **Nine** files pin the registry at 44 value entries and/or at ten ORBIT knobs — `test-cs018-p4`, `test-cs018-p6`, `test-cs018-p7`, `test-cs020-p1`, `test-cs020-p1b`, `test-cs021-p1`, `test-cs021-p1b`, `test-cs021-p3`, `test-cs022-p3` — on top of the three CS023 files that carry standing TRAPs naming P4 by name. Every one was flipped to its positive successor rather than deleted, and each records WHICH phase moved it, so the next changeset inherits a live pin rather than a hole: the counts become 46, the orbit-prefixed count becomes 11 (P3's ten plus `orbitGravityAccel`), and `test-cs021-p3`'s ORBIT-section membership check becomes a **prefix** claim plus an explicit "and the only ids after them are P4's two, appended and never inserted" — which is what append-only actually means and is a stronger guard than the equality it replaced. Two further repoints were consequences the spec DID predict: `test-cs023-p1`'s `ORBIT_FAST_RING` source-mention count **2 → 3**, named in §8's retirement ledger as "the one consumer, the one generator test, **and the drift's cap (C14)**"; and `test-cs023-p3`'s `debrisBounce` executable-line-count pin, repointed to compare both sides with P4's one specced `drifting` clear filtered out — deliberately NOT to "+1", which would have gone stale the instant this commit made `HEAD` equal the working file.

**Three files changed WITHOUT being touched, and that is the registry convention working.** `test-cs015-p5`, `test-cs015-p6` and `test-cs018-p2` sweep the registry GENERICALLY (min/max/step clamping, stepping, persistence round-trips) rather than by a fixed id list, so the two new knobs were picked up automatically and those files now run *more* assertions — `test-cs018-p2` went 691 → 711, all passing. No edit was needed or made.

**Two PRE-EXISTING intermittent failures were characterised rather than absorbed into the noise.** `test-cs017-p1` §F ("level 6: forcing the spawn timer produced exactly one saucer") failed on **both** pre-edit baseline runs, before a single line was changed; three standalone runs on the untouched tree gave two passes and one failure. `test-p5` §G ("one hooked canister spends exactly one piece") failed on the first post-edit run only; it was then run **20 times against a `git stash`-ed HEAD build carrying no P4 code whatsoever** and failed once there too. Both are unpinned-`Math.random()` sites in scenarios that predate this changeset; neither is caused by P4, and both should be pinned by whoever next touches those files.

**Baseline and regression, measured as instructed.** Before editing: **97 files**, one failing (`test-cs017-p1`, above), every other exit code 0; two baseline runs differed in exactly the seven documented RNG/wall-clock files. Three things P5 should carry that are NOT in its prompt: the `debrisBounceRestitution` wiring derivation above, the trigger-dominates-target finding above, and the fact that **moving `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS` or `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` now moves the drift's two radii as well as the ring geometry** — the same "this also moves the cap" warning its prompt already carries for `ORBIT_ANG_VEL`/`ORBIT_FAST_MULT`, one level up.

**This session (CS023 P3, landed):** implemented CS023 Phase 3 per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS023.md` §1.1/§4.3/§4.6, Correction C13, and FORK-CS023-E/F, re-grepping every P1/P2 anchor by symbol (both phases had moved things). Scope was the four phase-prompt items, the new test file, and the five-file repoint sweep the new mechanic's own scenarios required.

**A GENUINE DESIGN DECISION SURFACED AND WAS SURFACED RATHER THAN INVENTED, EXACTLY AS `CLAUDE.md` REQUIRES.** Before writing any code, `debrisBounce`'s free/free branch was confirmed (by reading the actual source, not by trusting the flag) to evaluate `DEBRIS_MASS[a.size]`/`DEBRIS_MASS[b.size]` — and `Saucer` (`class Saucer { constructor(small) { this.small = small; this.radius = ...; this.damage = ...; ... } }`) carries no `size` field at all, so `debrisBounce(a, s)` against a FREE satellite would compute `DEBRIS_MASS[undefined]` → `undefined` → `1/undefined` → `NaN` through the whole exchange, corrupting that satellite's velocity and position irrecoverably. Neither `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS023.md` §4.6 (which says `debrisBounce` "writes only to `a`" — already known false of the free/free branch, per P2's own §4.6 spec-inconsistency note) nor the P3 phase prompt assigns the saucer a mass. Asked directly via `AskUserQuestion` rather than picking an interpretation: **treat the saucer as immovable** (an infinite-mass wall the satellite bounces off; the alternative offered was "give saucers a finite mass on the `DEBRIS_MASS` scale," which would have required inventing tuning numbers nowhere in the spec). Paul chose immovable.

**THE RESOLUTION IS A ONE-LINE DISPATCH CHANGE IN `debrisBounce`, NOT A NEW BRANCH OR A SAUCER MASS CONSTANT.** `aRail`/`bRail` renamed to `aFixed`/`bFixed`: `const aFixed = !!a.orbitCenter || a instanceof Saucer, bFixed = !!b.orbitCenter || b instanceof Saucer;`. Every existing branch is reused unchanged in shape: a rail-borne satellite vs a saucer now takes the FIXED/FIXED no-op (the satellite is untouched, exactly spec §4.6's "a rail-borne satellite is untouched"), and a free satellite vs a saucer takes the FREE/FIXED branch with the satellite alone in the `f` role — reflecting off the saucer as if off a rail, floored at `DEBRIS_BOUNCE_MIN` since both bodies start coincident and at rest at the moment of contact (verified exactly: 40.000 px/s, direction from `angleTo`'s documented `atan2(0,0) === 0` zero-separation convention). The free/free branch — the one that would have NaN'd — is now provably unreachable for any saucer contact, because a saucer is always `aFixed`/`bFixed` by construction. No `SAUCER_MASS` constant, no special case in the UFO↔debris pass itself.

**THE OTHER THREE SITES ARE THE FOUR-ONE-LINERS CORRECTION C13 PROMISED.** In the hazards-vs-ship `else` branch: `damageShip` runs first (it reads `h.x`/`h.y` for the knockback vector), then `if (h instanceof HunterSatellite) destroyHunter(h, false); else destroyDebris(h, false);` — NOT gated on `damageShip`'s boolean return, because an auto-shield save returns `false` while the collision still physically happened (FLAG-CS023-k). The saucer sub-loop's `else` takes the matching `damageShip(s.damage, ...); destroySaucer(s, false);`. The `hazards` array stays the existing spread copy (split children pushed mid-iteration are not revisited this frame), the loop still never `break`s (shielded behaviour, out of scope per C10, is untouched), and `h.dead` is still checked at the top of each iteration — all three verified, not assumed. The UFO↔debris pass is P2's pair-walk idiom's sibling, one `for`-of over `game.saucers` × `game.debris`, `destroySaucer(s, false)` then `debrisBounce(a, s)` then `break` (one saucer, one death). `destroySaucer(s, awardScore = true)` guards `addScore` + `Achievements.lifetime.saucerKills`/`smallSaucerKills` + `game.stats.smallSaucerKills` and NOTHING else — `dropPowerup`/`boom`/`AudioSys.saucer` all sit textually AFTER the gate's closing brace, verified by slicing the function body and checking the drop calls fall outside it, mirroring `destroyHunter`'s large-tier `dropPowerup` placement exactly (TRAP 5's whole point).

**MULTI-OVERLAP IS THE I-FRAME, NOT A NEW RULE.** Three large debris satellites staged overlapping the ship on one frame: all three die (mutual damage fires for every overlapping hazard, exactly as the shielded path already deflects every overlapping hazard — same loop, same lack of `break`), but the ship loses exactly ONE `DEBRIS_DAMAGE[3]` worth of HP and `game.stats.dmgThisWave` increments exactly once, because `damageShip`'s OWN internal `s.invuln > 0` early-return blocks the second and third calls within the same frame — the destroy calls fire regardless (they are not gated on `damageShip`'s return), so all three still die, but only the first ever actually touches the hull. This is the existing i-frame mechanism doing new work, not new rate-limiting code.

**THE SHIELDED-RAM REGRESSION (C10) IS CHECKED FIELD-BY-FIELD AGAINST THE TRUE PRE-CS023 BASELINE, NOT AGAINST GEOMETRY THAT HAS SINCE MOVED.** A literal byte-identical comparison across builds would have been meaningless for any orbit-geometry-dependent scenario — P1 retuned every orbit radius and P2 added a whole bounce mechanism between `f9db5c2` and today. Section (D) instead drives three FIELD-level (geometry-independent) scenarios in both the current build and the pinned `f9db5c2` reference under identical staging: a free large debris satellite (`shieldDeflect`'s path), a medium homing Hunter (the shield-KILL path — `destroyHunter(h)`, single arg, in both builds), and a saucer (`destroySaucer(s)`, single arg, in both builds, proving the new second parameter's default leaves this call site behaviourally identical to before the parameter existed). All three come back field-identical (hp, energy, `deflects`, dead-flags, split counts, score) between the two builds — proof C10 holds across the WHOLE changeset, not just this diff.

**New `scratchpad/test-cs023-p3.js` (121 assertions, sections A–J, 0 failures, byte-identical across three consecutive runs)** drives the REAL `startGame`/`update(1/60)`/`damageShip`/`destroyDebris`/`destroyHunter`/`destroySaucer`/`debrisBounce` path; nothing under test is reimplemented. **(B)** all three ram targets (large debris, large Hunter, both saucer sizes) with `game.score`/every relevant achievement counter provably unchanged while canisters/the 3-way split/the large Hunter's powerup/the UFO's powerup all still appear. **(C)** a ram during i-frames does nothing at all. **(D)** the shielded-ram regression above. **(E)** the multi-overlap case above. **(F)** the auto-shield case: hull unchanged, `AUTO_SHIELD_SCORE_PENALTY` applied, hazard destroyed anyway. **(G)** UFO vs debris: the free-satellite bounce, the rail-borne no-op (orbit angle/position asserted EXACTLY as pure orbit motion, no bounce contribution), and a bullet-kill control proving score/stats still land through the unchanged path. **(H)** the C1 regression (UFO shots vs satellites, unchanged) pinned against `f9db5c2`. **(I)** determinism, the whole scenario running inside one seeded `Math.random()` stream (an early draft only seeded `startGame()` itself and produced a false failure here — the fix, not a game bug, is recorded in the test's own comment). **(J)** an `AudioSys.ctx === null` smoke test across a real 15-level ramp. **(A)** `node --check`, source pins for every new call site, the `aFixed`/`bFixed` rename, and TRAPs — including a diff-by-executable-line-count check that `debrisBounce` gained nothing beyond the documented rename, and raw-source (not comment-stripped) byte-identical pins on `shieldDeflect`/`shieldBounce`/`breakChain`/`scatterChain`/the whole hazards-vs-chain block against `HEAD`.

**THE REGRESSION SWEEP TOUCHED FIVE OTHER TEST FILES, ALL LEGITIMATE CONSEQUENCES OF THE NEW MECHANIC SURFACING IN SCENARIOS THAT PREDATE IT, NONE A BUG IN THIS PHASE'S OWN COLLISION CODE.** `test-cs023-p1.js` and `test-cs023-p2.js` each carried a standing TRAP asserting `destroySaucer` had NOT yet gained its `awardScore` parameter — both flipped to their positive successor, the mirror-image convention this codebase has used at every phase boundary since CS017 P6, rather than deleted. `test-cs023-p2.js` additionally had three assertions whose text-slicing anchors assumed nothing would ever sit between its own pass and either `debrisBounce`'s rail-dispatch line or the Cleanup section; P3's `aFixed`/`bFixed` rename and its new UFO↔debris pass (inserted directly between P2's pass and Cleanup, per spec §4.6's "beside P2's") broke those anchors without changing what they were actually verifying — repointed to track the renamed variables and to bound the "immediately before" slice against WHICHEVER pass comes next, so a future phase inserting its own pass here needs the identical repoint, not a redesign. `test-cs021-p1.js` §E (orbit motion through real frames) had a field-component satellite wander into the stationary ship over its 60-frame measurement window; P3 now destroys that satellite on contact, reordering `game.debris` and breaking the section's index-aligned before/after comparison — a scenario the section was never about (it measures orbit motion, not ram outcomes), fixed by pinning the ship's i-frame for the whole measurement. `test-cs017-p3.js` §F's `probe()` helper spawns a saucer via a pinned-`Math.random()` `update(0)` call while leftover debris from the section's own wave-staging is still on the field; the fresh saucer's (now-fixed, since random is pinned) spawn position could coincidentally overlap that debris, and P3's new UFO↔debris pass would destroy it and filter it out of `game.saucers` inside that same call — reporting `null` instead of the `true`/`false` the probe expects. Fixed by clearing debris/hunters before the probe, isolating the spawn-decision measurement from physics it was never testing. `test-f2.js` §b reused one persistent rammed satellite across two non-consecutive hits to test hit-stun timing; the first (now-mutual) ram destroys and splits it, so the second poke silently hit nothing — fixed with a fresh satellite, clearing the three leftover split children first since they land at the exact contact point and would otherwise apply the wrong tier's damage on the second hit.

**BASELINE MEASURED BEFORE EDITING: 95 files, 0 failures, every exit code 0. Full regression after, run twice consecutively: 96 files (the new P3 test added), 0 failures and 0 non-zero exits on both runs.** Both runs differ from baseline and from each other in exactly the same 7 files, all of them the already-documented unpinned-`Math.random()`/wall-clock noise (`test-cs018-p4`, `test-cs021-p1`'s §K probe, `test-cs022-p3`'s §H probe, `test-cs023-p2`'s own §H probe, `test-v31-world`, `test-v33-p2`, `test-v33-p3`) — every one still ends `0 failed`, and no new nondeterminism was introduced. TRAPs verified: `GAME_VERSION` unchanged at `"1.0.0.22"` (TRAP 1), no shielded-path edit of any kind — `shieldDeflect`, `shieldBounce`, `breakChain`, `scatterChain` pinned RAW-SOURCE byte-identical against `HEAD` (TRAPs 2/3), the registry still exactly 44 entries (TRAP 4), and `destroyHunter`'s large-tier `dropPowerup` call still sits OUTSIDE the `awardScore` gate, untouched (TRAP 5). It should re-grep every anchor this session moved, in particular `debrisBounce`'s renamed `aFixed`/`bFixed` (P4's one-line `drifting`-clear seam still drops in at the same place, just under the new names) and the two new collision passes' placement relative to Cleanup.

**Prior session (CS023 P2, landed):** implemented CS023 Phase 2 per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS023.md` §1.2/§4.4/§4.5/§4.8 and FORK-CS023-D, re-grepping every anchor by symbol (P1 had moved `ORBIT_FAST_RING`'s type and `generateOrbitLayout`'s parameter name, and the test file's generator arguments are assembled with `fastRingIndices` accordingly). Scope was the four phase-prompt items, the new test file, and the two mirror-image repoints P1's own STATUS entry predicted. **No genuine design decision surfaced that the spec does not settle** — but two implementation choices the spec's free/free row leaves open were DERIVED and are recorded below rather than left silent, and three spec inconsistencies were surfaced rather than interpreted.

**THREE EDITS IN `asteroids-deluxe.html`, AND THE HELPER IS MOST OF IT.** **(1) The constants block:** `DEBRIS_MASS`, `DEBRIS_BOUNCE_RESTITUTION`, `DEBRIS_BOUNCE_MIN`, inserted between `DEBRIS_GARBAGE` and `SAUCER_SCORE` so they sit INSIDE the `DEBRIS_*` group (asserted by source order, not by eye). **(2) `debrisBounce(a, b)`**, immediately after `shieldBounce`. **(3) The pass**, immediately before `--- Cleanup ---` in `update()`. Nothing else moved: `shieldBounce`, `shieldDeflect`, `coalesceGarbage`, `class Garbage`, `destroyDebris` and the entire hazards-vs-ship block are pinned byte-identical against `git show HEAD:` — this phase DERIVES from `shieldBounce`, it does not edit it, and P3 still owns the mutual-damage rule.

**THE FREE/RAIL BRANCH IS `shieldBounce` LINE FOR LINE, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** Outward normal from `angleTo(rail, free)`; relative velocity taken in the RAIL BODY'S frame; only the approaching component reflected; the floor applied last on the free body's world-frame outward component; the free body placed at `rail.radius + free.radius + 2` and `wrap()`ed. The rail body is never written. **Momentum is DELIBERATELY not conserved in this branch** — the rail is an infinite-mass constraint — and that is asserted in that direction with a control (it broke on 359 of 480 trials, i.e. essentially every trial where the free body was actually deflected), so nobody later "fixes" it into a symmetric exchange. The measured flavour result mirrors CS021 P1b's own: a rail satellite sweeping into a parked free satellite throws it at exactly **twice its own speed** — 83.8 px/s off ring 1, **338.0** off the inner fast ring, 141.6 off ring 3, **511.5** off the outer fast ring.

**TWO THINGS THE SPEC'S free/free ROW DOES NOT SPELL OUT WERE DERIVED FROM THE MASS-WEIGHTED RULE, NOT CHOSEN — recorded here because a future reader will want to know they were decisions.** Spec §4.4 describes free/free only as "elastic exchange along the contact normal, mass-weighted by `DEBRIS_MASS`, restitution `DEBRIS_BOUNCE_RESTITUTION`", and the separation floor and overlap push are described in the free/RAIL row, where the partner is immovable by construction. With two movable bodies neither of `shieldBounce`'s forms is even well defined. **(a) The floor is on the RELATIVE separation speed, applied as equal-and-opposite impulses**, so momentum survives the floor exactly as it survives the reflection — a per-body world-frame floor would break it, and "momentum conserved at the `DEBRIS_MASS` ratios" is the phase prompt's own test requirement in every case, not only the floor-idle ones. **(b) The overlap push is split in INVERSE proportion to mass** (heavier moves less), which is the positional statement of the same 9:1 ratio the impulse uses and is what FORK-CS023-D's "a small ricochets off a large rather than shoving it" means positionally. The free/RAIL branch keeps `shieldBounce`'s absolute placement byte-for-byte, because there the partner genuinely cannot move. Both choices are argued at the function in the source, and both are asserted as identities rather than inequalities — an earlier draft asserted "the heavier body moved less" with 1e-9 of slack and an even 50/50 split sailed through it.

**THE FLOOR EARNS ITS PLACE ON A CASE CS023 CREATES ON PURPOSE, WHICH IS WORTH RECORDING BECAUSE IT IS NOT THE CASE FLAG-CS023-f DESCRIBES.** `destroyDebris()` births its three children at the PARENT'S OWN POSITION, and a shattered rail-borne parent hands all three the SAME tangent (CS021 P1's FORK-C2 handoff) — so before this phase every orbit-level split produced three perfectly coincident bodies travelling as one, and after it they would have stayed that way forever without a floor. Driven for real in §G: the three children are provably coincident with identical velocities at birth, are apart after one frame, and are fully clear of contact after three. **A pair-walk resolves ONE pair at a time, so a three-body pile takes a few frames rather than one** — that is honest sequential-resolution behaviour, it is bounded and asserted at under 60 frames, and it is why the "separation within one frame" claim is stated per PAIR (where it holds exactly, on all 540 free/free trials and all 480 free/rail ones) rather than per pile.

**THE PHYSICS IS ASSERTED AS PHYSICS, AND THE SIZE ORDERING IS ASSERTED WITHOUT READING THE MASS TABLE AT ALL.** Over 60 incoming velocities for each of the nine ordered size pairs (540 free/free trials) and 60 per ring for two free sizes (480 free/rail trials): momentum conserved to 4.55e-13; tangential motion of both bodies EXACTLY preserved (worst drift 5.68e-14 px/s, because every impulse is along the normal); the separation speed equal to `max(restitution × approach speed, floor)` — Newton's restitution law, not the code's impulse expression; kinetic energy conserved to 2.33e-10 in every floor-idle case; a pair already separating faster than the floor left byte-identical; and geometric separation to exactly contact + 2 px in every single trial. A **sandbox build at restitution 0.5** carries the other half: energy strictly decreases, and by exactly `(1 − e²)` times the approach's reduced-mass kinetic energy. **The mass table itself needed a separate, table-free claim**: every assertion above builds its expectation FROM `DEBRIS_MASS`, so an inverted 1/3/9 table passes all of them — a head-on size-1 into a stationary size-3 must REVERSE while the size-3 leaves slower than the size-1 arrived, and two equal sizes must swap normal velocities exactly. **A tie between ADJACENT tiers is expected and is a property of the game's own split** rather than a coincidence: an elastic head-on gives `|v_light| = u(M−m)/(M+m)` and `v_heavy = 2mu/(M+m)`, equal exactly when `M = 3m`, and the 3-way split makes every adjacent tier exactly 3×. The strict inequality is asserted two tiers apart, at the 9:1 pair.

**THE ASYMMETRY IS PROVEN AGAINST A REAL CONTROL RUN, NOT AGAINST A RE-DERIVED RAIL LAW.** A free satellite is driven into a rail-borne one for 300 real `update(1/60)` frames, and the rail body's whole 300-frame trajectory — position, velocity and orbit angle — comes out **byte-identical to a control run in which the same satellite, constructed with the same RNG draws, is parked a third of a world away and never touches it**. Every non-kinematic field matches on every frame too; its distance to `orbitCenter` never leaves `orbitRadius` (worst 5.68e-13 px); and its orbit angle advances by exactly `orbitAngVel × dt` every frame. The twelve fields CS021 P1b §E pinned on a hazard across a `shieldBounce` are pinned again here across a direct `debrisBounce`. **The contact really happened on all 300 frames** — measured by the free body being deflected every one, not by final clearance: it ended geometrically clear of that particular ring satellite on ~273–286 of 300, because a full-ramp shell puts rings 138 px apart while a size-3 body is 92 px across, so a free body wedged between two rings can be resolved against one and land inside the other. Real behaviour of a sequential pair-walk, converging, reported rather than asserted at 100%.

**CORRECTION C11 IS PROVEN THREE WAYS AND THE FIRST DRAFT OF THE PROOF WAS WRONG.** The exhaustive sweep initially swept SAME-ring pairs' two angles independently, which models a pair that cannot exist — same-ring satellites are laid at a fixed angular spacing and share an angular velocity — and duly reported an 85 px overlap on ring 1 against itself. Split in two: **distinct** ring pairs swept 360×360 on the real torus (minimum **46.000 px**, on rings 676 and 814); **same-ring** pairs answered from the layout's own `actualGapPx` and from the real spawned bodies (746 px at the tightest, far wider). Plus 21 occurrences of real spawns, plus **1,800 real frames** of play whose measured minimum is exactly 46.0 px — so the corridor is genuinely reached in play and is genuinely the binding constraint. The rail/rail branch itself is forced directly and proved to be a literal no-op on all twelve fields of both bodies.

**WRAP CORRECTNESS CARRIES A LIVE NAIVE CONTROL, AND THE CONTROL'S FIRST STAGING DID NOT ACTUALLY STRADDLE A SEAM.** Twelve seam cases, each built so BOTH axes wrap (asserted, after the first attempt placed both bodies on the same side of a corner and the control passed for the wrong reason). The naive non-wrap normal points backwards on every one, and its distance measurement is wrong by up to **4,368 px**. Worth recording: at 3840×2160 the naive normal is **~164°** out, not 180° — the world is not square — so the control is stated as "a negative dot product", which is the actual claim, rather than as "cos ≈ −1".

**THREE SPEC INCONSISTENCIES, SURFACED RATHER THAN INTERPRETED. NONE IS A DESIGN FORK — §4.5 gives the pass's exact code and §4.4 its exact case table — but all three will mislead a later phase if they go unrecorded.** **(1) Spec §7 says "`WORLD_SIZE_FIELD` and every field level's play, which must come out byte-identical" — that is now FALSE and cannot be true of CS023.** The pass in §4.5 has no archetype gate and §1.1's "Debris ↔ Debris → elastic bounce" row has no level qualifier, so two free satellites bounce on a field level exactly as on an orbit one. It reads as a carry-over from CS022's §7, where the changeset genuinely was orbit-only. The spawn-level field byte-identity `test-cs023-p1.js` §I pins is unaffected (it snapshots the spawn, not driven frames) and still passes. **(2) Spec §4.6 says `debrisBounce` "writes only to `a`".** False of the shipped free/free branch, which must write both bodies to conserve momentum. Harmless for P3's use — the saucer is already `dead` by then — but P3 should not build on the sentence. **(3) THE SHARP ONE: a `Saucer` carries no `size` field** (`small`, `radius`, `damage`, `dead`, and no tier), so §4.6's `debrisBounce(a, s)` against a **free** satellite would evaluate `DEBRIS_MASS[undefined]` → `undefined` → `1/undefined` → **NaN through the whole free/free branch**, destroying that satellite's velocity and position irrecoverably. Against a RAIL-BORNE satellite it happens to work (the saucer becomes the "free" body and the rail is untouched, which is what §4.6 wants), so this would surface as an intermittent NaN rather than a crash. **P3 must decide the saucer's mass; §4.8's constants table does not give one, so it is a genuine gap and not something this phase should invent.**

**REPOINTS — TWO ASSERTIONS IN ONE FILE, EXACTLY AS P1's OWN STATUS ENTRY PREDICTED.** `test-cs023-p1.js` §I's "no `debrisBounce` helper yet (P2)" and "no `DEBRIS_MASS` table yet (P2)" TRAPs are repointed to their POSITIVE successors (`defined exactly once` / `declared exactly once`) rather than deleted — the standing mirror-image treatment, and keeping the positive form means that file goes on guarding against a second definition. The `drifting` / `ORBIT_GRAVITY_*` / `maxOrbitSpeed` / `destroySaucer`-parameter TRAPs are untouched and still hold. **Nothing else in the suite needed repointing**, which was checked by running the whole thing rather than by grepping: the only baseline-to-after stdout differences across 94 files are the six already-documented unpinned-`Math.random()`/wall-clock noise files.

**New `scratchpad/test-cs023-p2.js` (16,885 assertions, sections A–J, 0 failures, byte-identical across three consecutive runs apart from the wall-clock lines it labels `[REPORTED, never gated]`)** drives the REAL `startGame`/`nextWave`/`update(1/60)`/`destroyDebris`/`killShip`/`draw` path; nothing under test is reimplemented. **(A)** `node --check` plus source pins: the three constants declared once at spec §4.8's values and grouped inside the `DEBRIS_*` block by ORDER, mass conservation as arithmetic, neither `1.0` nor `40` inlined anywhere in the helper, `debrisBounce` defined once with NOTHING between it and `shieldBounce`, five functions and one class byte-identical to HEAD, the wrap-aware helpers asserted at the SITE with three `wrap()` calls, the rail gate reading `orbitCenter`, the pass's live-array walk and its placement between `game.debris.forEach` / the chain scan and `--- Cleanup ---` with only closing braces in between, both dead-flag checks, TRAP 3's ten forbidden references inside the helper and nine inside the pass, and the docs untouched via `git diff --name-only`. **(B)** the free/free physics above, plus the table-free size ordering and the restitution-0.5 sandbox. **(C)** the free/rail physics, both argument orders proved equivalent, and the deliberate momentum break. **(D)** the asymmetry against a real control run. **(E)** C11. **(F)** the seam. **(G)** the pass through real frames: one frame separates an overlapped pair on a FIELD level; C12's predicted fix (an overlapped field satellite pushed clear of a ring satellite that stays on its rail); dead bodies skipped in BOTH array orders; garbage canisters held out of coalescence and proved unbounced; and the coincident-split case. **(H)** the gate. **(I)** determinism. **(J)** an `AudioSys.ctx === null` update-and-draw smoke over 720 frames across the whole ramp and a field level.

**MUTATION-TESTED AGAINST TWENTY DELIBERATE BREAKAGES, EVERY ONE CAUGHT — and three of them only after the suite was strengthened, which is the part worth recording.** Caught first time: rail gate removed (2,266 failures, sections ACDEFG), free/free reflecting the receding component (1,617), free/rail the same (86), free/free floor removed (188), free/rail floor removed (58), mass ignored entirely (1,662), free/free naive normal (49), free/rail naive normal (10), free/free overlap push removed (1,095), free/free writing only `a` (2,456 — the spec §4.6 wording taken literally), the rail body pushed too (990), restitution → 0 (237), and the 2 px epsilon removed (817). **Three slipped and were fixed:** **DEBRIS_MASS inverted to 1/3/9** was caught by a source pin alone (3 failures) because every physics assertion built its expectation from the table — now 13 failures including behavioural ones, via the table-free size-ordering block; **the overlap push split 50/50** was caught by NOTHING (0 failures) because the "heavier moves less" assertion carried 1e-9 of slack that equality satisfies — now 540 failures, via the exact `ma × moveA === mb × moveB` identity; and **the pass's inner dead-flag check removed** was caught by a source pin alone (1 failure) because §G staged the dead body FIRST, where the OUTER guard catches it — now 5, and the outer guard's own mutant is caught too (6).

**BASELINE MEASURED BEFORE EDITING: 94 files, 0 failures, every exit code 0**, captured as full per-file stdout+exit-code snapshots. **Full regression after, run twice consecutively: 95 files (the new P2 test added), 0 failures and 0 non-zero exits on both runs, 36,147 assertions both times.** Run-to-run: 6 files differ, all of them the already-documented unpinned-`Math.random()`/wall-clock noise (`test-cs021-p1`'s §K probe, `test-cs022-p3`'s §H probe, `test-cs023-p2`'s own §H probe, `test-v31-world`, `test-v33-p2`, `test-v33-p3`) — and in all three frame-budget probes the differing lines are the **timings only**, with every pair-check counter byte-identical, which is exactly why the gates are asserted on counters. Baseline-to-after: 6 files differ, and they are the same noise set plus `test-cs018-p4`'s documented RNG-dependent assertion count (424 → 422) — **no test file's asserted content changed**, including `test-cs023-p1`, whose two repointed assertions moved from stderr FAIL lines to silent passes. `test-cs022-p3`'s own gate is unaffected and still passes: its coalesce measurement moved 36,816 → 33,901, *down*, because bouncing debris spreads the garbage it later emits. TRAPs verified: `GAME_VERSION` unchanged at `"1.0.0.22"` (TRAP 1), `DEBUG_ENTRIES` still exactly 44 with no `debrisBounceRestitution` (TRAP 4), `coalesceGarbage`/`Garbage` byte-identical (TRAP 2), no ship or saucer path and no `damageShip` call anywhere in the new code (TRAP 3), and `git status` showing only `asteroids-deluxe.html`, `STATUS.md`, the one repointed test file and the one new one — the GDD, `GDD-VERSION-HISTORY.md` and `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` are untouched (P5's job). Committed per `CLAUDE.md`'s commit-per-phase rule; did **not** push. **Next: CS023 P3** — mutual collision damage. It must settle the saucer-mass gap above before it writes `debrisBounce(a, s)`.

**Prior session (CS023 P1, landed):** implemented CS023 Phase 1 per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS023.md` §1.3/§1.4/§4.1/§4.2 and Corrections C2/C3/C4/C5/C6/C7/C8, with FORK-CS023-A and FORK-CS023-G resolved. Scope was the six phase-prompt items, the new test file, and the single repoint sweep for the whole changeset. **No genuine design decision surfaced** — the spec settled every fork this phase touches — but four things are flagged below that the prompt did not predict, two of which are silent-degeneration cases a green suite would never have shown.

**THE EXECUTABLE DIFF IS NINE LINES, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE PHASE.** `git diff` over `asteroids-deluxe.html` with comment lines filtered out is exactly: the four constant values (`WORLD_SIZE_ORBIT` 16 → 9, `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS` 460 → 400, `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` 276 → 138, `ORBIT_DENSITY` → flat 0.12), `ORBIT_FAST_RING`'s type change (`3` → `[2, 4]`), `generateOrbitLayout`'s parameter rename (`fastRingIndex` → `fastRingIndices`) plus its one-line `const fastIdx = fastRingIndices || [];` normalisation and the membership test that replaces `i === fastRingIndex`, `activeRingsFor`'s fill loop (`rings.push(i)` for `rings.push(count - 1 - i)`), and `spawnOrbitWave`'s call site (`ORBIT_FAST_RING.map(n => n - 1)`). **`levelDef` is byte-untouched in its entirety**, as are `orbitGapMult`, `ORBIT_GAP_MULT`/`_FLOOR`/`_STEP`, `ORBIT_SAFETY_MARGIN`, `ORBIT_ANG_VEL`, `ORBIT_FAST_MULT`, `ORBIT_SPAWN_TRIES` and `WORLD_SIZE_FIELD` (TRAP 4). Everything else in the diff is comment, and the comments are most of the work: the fitted-radii paragraph rewritten around 860-against-1,060, the corridor facts stated, the density rhythm sentence **deleted rather than amended**, C5's finding written into the `ORBIT_GAP_MULT` block, the ramp's whole rationale block rewritten (it argued outermost-first throughout), `resizeWorld`'s stale grow comment corrected per C7, and the two now-false array-position notes fixed at **both** their source sites.

**THE TWO NOW-FALSE NOTES WERE AT DIFFERENT PLACES THAN THE PROMPT IMPLIED, AND BOTH ARE CORRECTED IN PLACE RATHER THAN DELETED.** CS022 P3's claim that *"a ring's array position in `layout.rings` is no longer its ring index"* lives in the source at **(1)** the `activeRingsFor` rationale block and **(2)** `generateOrbitLayout`'s `outerEdge` comment, which said the edge was *"1,334 px at every occurrence, since the ramp fills from the outside in."* Innermost-first makes `rings[k].index === k` again at every occurrence, and it also makes the outer edge **grow** — 446 / 584 / 722 / 860 px across occurrences 1–4 — where it used to be constant. Both sites now say so explicitly and say that the old note is false, because a future reader finding one corrected site and one silent one would trust the wrong one. The test file pins the corrective framing itself (`REVERSES WHAT THIS USED TO SAY`, `CORRECTS WHAT THIS COMMENT USED TO CLAIM`), not merely the absence of the retired text — a first draft pinned the absence and was satisfied by a comment that quietly dropped the claim instead of correcting it.

**THE §1.4 TABLE REPRODUCES EXACTLY, THROUGH A REAL `nextWave()`, AT EVERY ORBIT LEVEL 3 → 63, GROUPED BY `orbitRadius` AND NEVER BY ARRAY POSITION.** Level 3 = 3 ring + 5 field = 8; 6 = 6+3 = 9; 9 = 11+13 = 24; 12 = 16+9 = 25; 15 = 16+5 = 21; 18 = 16+3 = 19; 21 = 16+13 = **29**. From level 12 the ring half is frozen at 16 and the total breathes 19 / 21 / 25 / 29 with the junk cycle, peaking at 29 on levels 21, 30, 42, 51 and 63 — the same peak levels as CS022's, which is the junk cycle's doing rather than the shell's. Lanes are 746 / 1035 / 970 / 931 px, every one of them over 700; ring 1 is still the tightest. Tangential speeds are 41.9 / **169.0** / 70.8 / **255.7** px/s, the two bold ones being the fast rings.

**C5 IS ASSERTED AS ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN RESTATED AS PROSE, AND THE RESULT IS EXACTLY WHAT THE SPEC PREDICTS.** Walking the full four-ring shell through the real generator at all 21 occurrences and collecting every place a ring's count changes yields **exactly one step**: ring 2, occurrence 3, 3 → 4. The mechanism it belongs to is provably still live — `maxCount` really does widen 16/21/27/32 → 18/24/30/36 across the curve, ~13% — it is just no longer observable in a count. The full shell is 15 satellites at occurrences 1–2 and 16 from occurrence 3 through level 63.

**THE FAST-RING LIST IS EXERCISED AT LENGTHS 0, 1, 3 AND 4 IN A SANDBOX, AND C14's OWN COUNTEREXAMPLE IS A LIVE CONTROL.** An empty list makes no ring fast and does not throw; an absent list behaves as empty (matching what the retired scalar did when undefined); out-of-range indices match nothing and do not wrap onto a real ring. At the shipped `[2, 4]` the fastest satellite happens to be on ring 4 (255.7 px/s), but the test also builds the `[1, 2]` case the spec names and asserts the maximum falls on **ring 2** (169.0 px/s) rather than the outermost ring's 85.2 — so P4's speed cap cannot be written as "just use the outer ring" and pass.

**THE RAMP DIRECTION IS PROVEN BEHAVIOURALLY, NOT BY REGEX, AGAINST A PINNED-SHA CS022 REFERENCE MODULE.** From the same call, level 3 lays one ring at `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS` on this build and one ring at `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS + 3 × ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` on the reference (`f9db5c2` — a FIXED SHA, never `HEAD`, and its `asteroids-deluxe.html` is byte-identical to CS022 P4's `b62d950` and to the public HEAD `6654ef6` the spec names, since the intervening commit touched only the two planning documents). The reference is identified positively (inner radius 460, step 276, world size 16, `ORBIT_FAST_RING` the scalar 3, `GAME_VERSION` 1.0.0.22) so a bad SHA cannot pass quietly. `layout.rings[k].index === k` is asserted at all 21 occurrences here **and shown to have been false there**, which is what makes the claim worth making at all; the outer edge is asserted as `[446, 584, 722, 860]` here against a flat `[1334, 1334, 1334, 1334]` on the reference.

**REPOINT SWEEP — SIXTEEN FILES, AND THE FOUR-ROUNDS-RUNNING PATTERN HELD A FIFTH TIME.** **Eight failed outright and were repointed:** `test-cs021-p1` (89 failures — the §1.3 geometry table, the un-ramped total 65 → 15, the outer edge, the budget, the fast-ring block, the ramp cross-check), `test-cs021-p1b` (3, one of them a hard crash), `test-cs021-p2` (20 — the maxCount/count tables at both ends of the occurrence curve, and the "and it is the OUTERMOST one" ramp assertion), `test-cs021-p3` (25 — §F's whole clamp boundary moved out by a ring, §K's fast ring, §L's totals table), `test-cs022-p1` (10), `test-cs022-p2` (31 — §D's role reverted to what P2 wrote it for: the cross-validated helper at a NON-live geometry), `test-cs022-p3` (75 — its §B ramp table, its §D halving section, its §F/§G/§H budget, world dims and gate validity), `test-v31-world` (2). **Four passed but were silently mis-wired, and were fixed anyway** — `test-cs017-p1`, `test-cs017-p2`, `test-cs017-p3` and `test-cs018-p3` each carry a private `orbitTotalAt()` copy still passing the retired scalar key `fastRingIndex:`. The generator now ignores an unknown key, so `fastIdx` was `[]` and **every ring in those local layouts was silently slow**. Those helpers only read `.total`, so nothing failed and nothing ever would have — a wiring mismatch that means nothing today and would mislead the first person to read an `angVel` off one. **Four more were comment-only accuracy fixes** (`test-cs017-p5`, `test-cs020-p1`, `test-cs020-p1b`, `test-f5`): all four carry live guards that read `worldDims(game.worldSize)` and pass unchanged, but their prose still said the orbit torus was 5120×2880.

**THE SHARPEST REPOINT IS THE ONE THE PROMPT'S TRAP (b) NAMED, AND IT CRASHED RATHER THAN DEGRADED.** `test-cs021-p1b.js` §K parked the ship on `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS + (ORBIT_RING_COUNT − 1) × ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` at level 3 — correct while the ramp filled outermost-first (CS022 P3 laid ring 4 alone at occurrence 1), and under the inversion that lookup finds nothing and the run dies on `sat.x`. It now reads the ring off the level's OWN `activeRingsFor()` set, so neither ramp direction nor any future ring count can break it again. **The quieter sibling in the same file is the one worth recording:** §C and §E hardcoded ring index 2 as "the fast ring", which was true at `ORBIT_FAST_RING = 3` and is false at `[2, 4]` — index 2 is a SLOW ring now, so the "the fast ring shoves a parked ship far harder than a slow one" comparison was measuring one slow ring against another. Both builds' fast and slow rings are now resolved by ROLE from each build's own `ORBIT_FAST_RING` (normalised across the scalar/list type difference), so the section compares what it says it compares — 41.9 px/s throwing the ship at the 120 px/s floor against 169.0 px/s throwing it at 338.0.

**FOUR THINGS SURPRISED ME.** **(1) The `orbitEffectiveCount` clamp moved out by a ring and is now unreachable from the debug panel.** C6 predicted a requested 5 would be accepted; what falls out with it is that the registry's `orbitCount` entry has `max: 5`, so **nothing a player can dial through the panel clamps any more** and `clampShown` — CS021 P3's one bespoke registry hook — is only observable past the registry's own ceiling. It is still proven there (a requested 6 walks to 5, and `applyDebug` does not pre-clamp to `max`, so the persistence round-trip still has real teeth), and a five-ring request now genuinely spawns five rings and completes at occurrence 5. Recorded because "the hook has no reachable effect" is a fact a future reader should not have to discover. **(2) At level 3 the orbit level is now the LIGHTER load.** `test-cs021-p1.js` §K's frame-budget probe compares a level-3 orbit wave against the densest field level (junkCount 13); through CS021 and CS022 the orbit side was unambiguously heavier (40, then 27 satellites), and it is now 8. The validity assertion is repointed in its new direction rather than deleted, so a future re-inflation of the shell fails there and gets looked at. **(3) The frame-budget gate's blitz control fell below its own literal.** `test-cs022-p3.js` §H asserted the counter "can register millions of checks" against a bare `> 1,000,000`; the ~5× shell cut took the deliberately-unreachable blitz from 8.27M to **975,046**. The claim that literal ever meant is "far more than the gate allows", so it is now stated against `HARVEST_PAIR_CEILING` itself. **Neither ceiling is tightened** — STATUS's standing Known-issues note says they are derived from arithmetic rather than fitted to a measurement, and the gate's slack (36,816 checks against 8,000,000, 0.46%) is enormous now precisely BECAUSE §1.3 cut the shell. **(4) Five files' assertion counts fell sharply and no section disappeared.** `test-cs021-p1` 3745 → 2773, `test-cs022-p3` 2987 → 2317, `test-cs021-p3` 561 → 365, `test-cs017-p3` 1433 → 1378, `test-cs017-p1` 600 → 458. Every one is per-satellite and per-ring loops running over a 5× smaller shell; the section lists are byte-identical before and after, checked directly rather than assumed.

**New `scratchpad/test-cs023-p1.js` (637 assertions, sections A–K, 0 failures, byte-identical across three consecutive runs)** covers spec §6 items 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 19 and 20 and drives the REAL `startGame`/`nextWave`/`update(1/60)`/`draw` path; nothing under test is reimplemented, and every expectation is recomputed from the same `generateOrbitLayout` + `activeRingsFor` + `levelDef` + `worldDims` the shipped code is wired to. Every `build()` runs inside a seeded LCG, because the starfield is laid down with `Math.random()` at module load — named explicitly in §J as the one unpinned site. **(A)** `node --check` plus source pins for every constant this phase moves AND every one it does not, the corridor facts asserted as derivations (138 IS 1.5 satellite diameters; 400 − 46 − 88 IS 266; the 46 px corridor IS narrower than the 65 px floor), the deleted rhythm sentence in **both** wordings it ever had, C5's finding present in the `ORBIT_GAP_MULT` block with its don't-retune/don't-delete instruction, the ramp block arguing innermost-first and nowhere outermost-first, both array-position corrections, the executable fill loop with trailing comments stripped (a first draft was fooled by `// (was: count - 1 - i)` on the same line), and C7's corrected `resizeWorld` comment. **(B)** the §1.4 table at every orbit level. **(C)** the counts through the generator plus C5 as arithmetic. **(D)** the fast-ring list by ring index at every occurrence including the one with no fast ring, the four sandbox lengths, C14's counterexample, and — added after a mutant slipped through — the angular velocities read off REAL entities from a REAL `nextWave()`, because everything else in the section feeds the generator arguments this file assembles and cannot see a break in `spawnOrbitWave`'s own wiring. **(E)** the ramp direction against the pinned reference. **(F)** world size across 30 real transitions, `dmax` 1020/660, and C7's band arithmetic. **(G)** the budget and the C6 clamp, asserted as a CHANGE against the reference build. **(H)** C8's star derivation. **(I)** all four TRAPs, including eight real FIELD waves byte-identical to the pre-P1 build under one shared seed with an orbit-level control that must differ (8 here, 27 there). **(J)** determinism. **(K)** an `AudioSys.ctx === null` update-and-draw smoke across the whole ramp. **Mutation-tested against eleven deliberate breakages, every one caught:** ramp reverted to outermost-first (60 failures), step back to 276 (111), inner radius back to 460 (44), density back to CS022's curve (41), fast-ring membership reduced to the first entry only (25), `WORLD_SIZE_ORBIT` back to 16 (24), fast ring back to a one-element list (8), the ramp ignoring the `orbitCount` knob (2), the call site sending the retired scalar key (4 — one source pin and three behavioural, the three added specifically because the first draft caught it with the source pin alone), the corridor fact deleted from the constants block (1), and the rhythm sentence reinstated (1).

**BASELINE MEASURED BEFORE EDITING: 93 files, 0 failures, every exit code 0**, captured as full per-file stdout+exit-code snapshots. **Full regression after, run twice consecutively: 94 files (the new P1 test added), 0 failures and 0 non-zero exits on both runs.** Run-to-run: **5 files differ, all of them the already-documented unpinned-`Math.random()`/wall-clock noise** (`test-cs021-p1`'s §K probe, `test-cs022-p3`'s §H probe, `test-v31-world`, `test-v33-p2`, `test-v33-p3`) — and in both frame-budget probes the differing lines are the *timings only*, with every pair-check counter byte-identical, which is exactly why the gate is asserted on the counter. `test-cs018-p4`'s documented RNG-dependent assertion count happened to land identically on both runs this time.
