**Prior session (CS022 P3, landed):** implemented CS022 Phase 3 per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS022.md` §1.2/§1.3/§1.4/§4.4/§4.5/§4.6/§4.7 and FORK-CS022-E/F/G, re-grepping every P1/P2 anchor by symbol (both phases had moved things). Scope was the six phase-prompt items, the new test file, and the repoint sweep. **No genuine design decision surfaced** — the spec settled every fork this phase touches — but four things are flagged below that the prompt did not predict, one of which materially changes how the frame-budget ceiling should be read.

**SIX EDITS IN `asteroids-deluxe.html`.** **(1) The constants block:** `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS` 180 → 460, `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` 150 → 276, `ORBIT_DENSITY` `[0.75,0.45,0.35,0.85]` → `[0.75,0.45,0.35,0.42]`, with the fitted-radii paragraph rewritten around the 1,334-vs-1,420 budget at `WORLD_SIZE_ORBIT` and the density rhythm line rewritten to *tight → breather → widest → wide* (Correction C7). `ORBIT_ANG_VEL`/`ORBIT_FAST_MULT`'s px/s figures moved with the radii (~135 px/s at ring 4, ~318 at the fast ring) — a factual consequence of the constants this phase moved, not a scope widening. **(2) `ORBIT_RING_COUNT` RELOCATED** out of the `ORBIT_*` geometry block and down beside `ORBIT_LEVEL_EVERY` in the level-table block — the value and meaning are unchanged; see the FLAG-CS022-e paragraph below for why it had to move. **(3) `generateOrbitLayout` gains `activeRings`** and a new returned `inactive` array. **(4) `activeRingsFor(level)`** beside `orbitGapMult`. **(5) `levelDef`** gains `orbitRings` and `fieldCount` (14 → 16 columns), with `archetype`/`junkCount` hoisted out of the object literal into locals because three columns now read them. **(6) `spawnFieldSatellites(n, speedMul)`** extracted verbatim + `nextWave()`'s branch rewired; `spawnOrbitWave` takes a third `activeRings` argument.

**THE GEOMETRY REPRODUCES SPEC §4.7 EXACTLY, THROUGH A REAL `nextWave()`, AT EVERY ORBIT LEVEL 3 → 63.** Ring identity is asserted by `orbitRadius`, never by array position. Level 3 = 22 ring + 5 field = 27; 6 = 37+3 = 40; 9 = 52+13 = 65; 12 = 67+9 = 76; 15 = 70+5 = 75; 18 = 70+3 = 73; 21 = 71+13 = **84**. From level 24 the ring half is frozen at 71 and the total breathes 74 / 76 / 80 / 84 with the junk cycle, peaking at 84 on levels 21, 30, 42, 51 and 63. Un-ramped (all four rings at the base 2.5× multiplier) the layout is 65 satellites with lanes 114 / 238 / 332 / 276 px and maxCounts 18 / 29 / 40 / 51; at the 1.8 floor it is 71 with maxCounts 20 / 33 / 45 / 58. Ring 1's clearance over the permanent 88 px dock is **326 px**, against CS021's 46.

**THE HALVING IS EXACT AT OCCURRENCE 1 AND HALF-A-SATELLITE OFF AT THE FLOOR, AND THE TEST SAYS SO RATHER THAN PARROTING THE SPEC.** FORK-CS022-G calls 0.85 → 0.42 "an exact halving at both ends," and at level 3 it is: 22 × 2 === 44, asserted with `===`. At the 1.8 floor it is 25 against 49, not 50 — because `count = round(1 + density × (maxCount − 1))` halves the *(count − 1)* term, not `count`, so the final rounding can land half a satellite either side. The suite asserts `now === Math.round(old / 2)` at both ends, `now * 2 === old` at the exact one, and computes both against the retired 0.85 through the real generator rather than restating "22 vs 44" — so a future density retune fails loudly. The peak the halving exists to hold down is pinned both ways too: 84 with 0.42, **108** with 0.85.

**FLAG-CS022-e COST A CONSTANT RELOCATION, EXACTLY AS THE PRECEDENT SAID IT WOULD.** `levelDef`'s new `orbitRings` column clamps against `ORBIT_RING_COUNT`, and `test-cs018-p1.js` §B slices the level-table block from its banner to `levelDef`'s closing brace and evaluates it in a bare `vm` context with nothing else in scope — so `ORBIT_RING_COUNT` had to move INSIDE that slice, precisely as `ORBIT_LEVEL_EVERY` did in CS021 P1 and for the identical reason. A pointer comment marks its old home. The recursion itself survives the slice and terminates by construction — verified at 0, −3, 63, 66, 500, 1e6, `Infinity` and `NaN`, all at most one level deep, because `n − 1` is never an orbit level when `n` is and `levelDef(Infinity)` resolves to `"field"` (`NaN % 3 !== 0`). `test-cs018-p1.js` §B now pulls both constants out of the sandbox as direct proof they are inside it.

**⛔ THE FRAME-BUDGET GATE PASSES — AND FOUND THAT ITS OWN CEILING WAS DERIVED FROM A LOAD THE GAME CANNOT PUT THROUGH THE PASS.** The gate counts `coalesceGarbage`'s inner-loop iterations in an instrumented copy of the real source (one increment, one site, pinned) — machine-independent and byte-identical run to run, which is the whole point of gating on a counter rather than on GC-noisy headless wall time. Two ceilings, both derived BEFORE measuring: **8,000,000** for the worst case (CS021 §K's 1,233 canisters ≈ 760k pair-checks, × 2.10 for 84-vs-40 satellites, × 1.246 for CS022 P1's measured size-16 standing-garbage increase → ~3,226 canisters ≈ 5.20M, plus a 1.54× margin for the pair count's quadratic sensitivity) and a tighter **500,000** for the realistic path (CS022 P1's measured 319 live-harvest canisters × 2.10 → ~670 ≈ 224k, × 2.2). **Measured at level 21, the 84-satellite peak: a real progressive full harvest peaks at 49,203 pair-checks over 337 standing canisters — 0.62% of the worst-case budget and 9.8% of the realistic one — with a 0.67 ms median `update(dt)` (p95 1.12, p99 1.49) and 1,158 peak entities. A real death detonation measures ZERO.** Both gates pass with room to spare. **The finding: `updateDeath()` does not call `coalesceGarbage` at all, and `killShip()` is terminal (F2 — no respawn, no lives counter anywhere), so the 4,369-canister death-detonation peak the ceiling was derived from has never gone through the O(n²) pass on a single frame in the shipped game and structurally cannot.** The only other mass-destruction event, the SMD's Hunter sweep, deliberately sets `game.sweepPause` and suspends coalescence for the same reason. All four facts are source-pinned. A third, deliberately-unreachable **BLITZ** probe (every satellite and split child destroyed in a handful of frames with the ship kept alive) puts that load through the live pass anyway and hits **8,273,707 checks and a 372 ms frame** — which validates the derivation's order of magnitude (projected 5.20M), shows exactly what a spatial hash would buy, and doubles as the counter's teeth-proof. It is REPORTED, never gated on, because it is not a state the game can enter.

**New `scratchpad/test-cs022-p3.js` (2,987 assertions, sections A–L, 0 failures, byte-identical across runs apart from the wall-clock lines it explicitly labels as reported-not-gated)** drives the REAL `startGame`/`nextWave`/`update(1/60)`/`destroyDebris`/`killShip`/`draw` path; nothing under test is reimplemented, and every expectation is recomputed from the same `generateOrbitLayout` + `activeRingsFor` + `levelDef` the shipped code is wired to. The two deliberate literal pins are spec §4.7's published totals and the retired 0.85 density, both named as such. **(A)** `node --check` plus source pins: the three moved constants and the six that did not, both radii proven to BE 5× and 3× the satellite diameter rather than free numbers, the rewritten comment paragraphs, `ORBIT_RING_COUNT`'s position inside the level-table block by ORDER, the ring filter's placement after `radius` and before `maxCount`, `inactive` being its own array and never `rejected`, `activeRingsFor` reading the EFFECTIVE count, both `spawnFieldSatellites` call sites, the generator still unaware of occurrence, the single recursive call inside `levelDef`'s own body, and all three TRAPs. **(B)** the §4.7 table at every orbit level, grouped by `orbitRadius`, including that the rings present are always the OUTERMOST *n* and that ring 4 is always among them. **(C)** the field component: count, `levelDef(n−1)` provenance, absence of all four orbit fields, and every one of 100+ satellites inside the real `[SPAWN_MIN_DIST, SPAWN_MAX_DIST]` ring by wrap-aware `dist2`. **(D)** the halving. **(E)** the extraction: a text pin against the FIXED pre-P3 commit `ae60720` (never `HEAD` — the CS017 P6 trap) plus the stronger behavioural pin — eight real FIELD-level waves BYTE-IDENTICAL to the pre-P3 build under one seed, with an orbit-level control that must differ. **(F)** geometry guards at the orbit world size, at all 21 occurrences. **(G)** wrap correctness at 5120×2880 across seven edge/corner docks plus a real seam-side spawn, with a naive-arithmetic control off by 5,197 px. **(H)** the gate. **(I)** all 42 field levels. **(J)** determinism. **(K)** `levelDef`'s columns and the recursion. **(L)** `AudioSys.ctx` null smoke across a real ramp.

**REPOINT SWEEP — TWELVE FILES, AGAINST THE PROMPT'S PREDICTED FIVE, AND THE FOUR-TIMES-RUNNING PATTERN HELD.** The prompt named `test-cs017-p1/-p2/-p3`, `test-cs018-p3` and `test-cs021-p1`; the sweep found seven more. **The `orbitTotalAt` five, as predicted:** all four copies of the helper (`test-cs017-p1/-p2/-p3`, `test-cs018-p3`) now pass `activeRings: A.activeRingsFor(level)` and return `ringTotal + levelDef(level).fieldCount`, and `test-cs021-p1.js`'s equivalent became `expectedOrbitSpawn()` returning both halves. **The C6 reversal, as predicted:** `test-cs021-p1.js` §D and `test-cs017-p2.js` §B asserted orbit levels do NOT consume `junkCount`; both now assert the exact opposite, split into the two populations. **The seven the prompt did not predict, every one of them a case where "which rule applies" stopped being a per-LEVEL question and became a per-ENTITY one:** `test-cs017-p1.js` §F fed the field component's `undefined orbitAngVel` into the rail-speed rule and produced `NaN`; `test-cs017-p3.js` §B asserted every orbit-level satellite carries orbit state; `test-v31-world.js` §B did the same to the ring-radius check; `test-cs018-p1.js` §G (key count 14 → 16, `orbitRings` added to the UNCLAMPED list, `fieldCount` deliberately NOT — it IS plateau-identical) and §I (the self-recursive `levelDef(n − 1)` needed a narrow, exact-text, paid-for exemption in the every-reader-passes-`game.wave` scan); `test-cs021-p1b.js` staged hazards by hardcoded ring RADIUS at level 3 and crashed outright, so its cases now name a ring by INDEX and each build — the CS022 one and the pinned CS021 P1 reference — resolves that index against its own geometry and its own ramp; `test-cs021-p2.js` §B compared angular-velocity SETS at levels 3 and 24, which is one ring against three now; `test-cs021-p3.js` §I keyed a ring snapshot by ARRAY POSITION where the ramp makes position and ring index diverge (a crash, not a soft failure), §F/§G/§K staged at level 3 expecting every requested ring on the board, and §L pinned the 40/45/45 totals. **`test-cs022-p1.js` and `test-cs022-p2.js` got the standing mirror-image treatment** on their own "P3 owns this" TRAPs. **Two more that were passing but measuring the wrong thing, fixed anyway:** `test-cs021-p1.js` §C budgeted the outer edge against `WORLD_H/2 − 20` from the load-time FIELD snapshot (700 px) — the same class CS022 P1 caught in `test-cs021-p2.js` §D, and it survived only because CS021's 676 px edge happened to clear 700 as well; §H and §I placed things at ring radii around a hardcoded 1280/720 centre in the field world, which stops meaning anything the moment a radius exceeds 720. **Reviewed and left alone, with the reason:** the other files that reach an orbit level (`test-cs018-p4/-p5/-p7`, `test-f5`, `test-f9`, `test-cs017-p4/-p5`, `test-v31-coalesce`, `test-cs020-p1/-p1b`) all clear `game.debris` around their staging and make no population-dependent claim — established by instrumenting a throwaway copy of the build to report which levels each test file actually reaches, rather than by reading staging calls and hoping.

**BASELINE MEASURED BEFORE EDITING: 92 files, 0 failures, every exit code 0**, captured as full per-file stdout+exit-code snapshots. **Full regression after, run twice consecutively: 93 files (the new P3 test added), 0 failures and 0 non-zero exits on both runs, 20,647 assertions both times.** Run-to-run: **5 files differ, all of them the already-documented unpinned-`Math.random()`/wall-clock noise** (`test-cs021-p1`'s §K probe, `test-cs022-p3`'s §H probe, `test-v31-world`, `test-v33-p2`, `test-v33-p3`) — and in both frame-budget probes the differing lines are the *timings only*; every pair-check counter is byte-identical, which is exactly why the gate is asserted on the counter. Baseline-to-after: 16 files differ — the 12 repointed above, plus `test-v33-p2`/`test-v33-p3` (RNG display), `test-cs020-p1b` (a reported frame count in a non-asserted console line; the RNG stream moved because levels 3/6/9/12 spawn different counts now), and `test-cs017-p5`, whose only change is that a `orbit spawn safety: ring 4 exhausted 60 rerolls` warning **stopped firing** — a real consequence of the new geometry, since at 460/736/1012/1288 only ring 1 can fall inside the `[DOCK_MIN_DIST, DOCK_MAX_DIST]` band the ship's distance to the dock lives in, and it clears easily. `test-cs018-p4`'s documented RNG-dependent assertion count re-measured at 422/424 over eight isolated runs (always 0 failures; the recorded spread was 403–423 and shifts whenever the orbit spawn changes the RNG stream, exactly as CS021 P1 recorded). TRAPs verified: `GAME_VERSION` unchanged at `"1.0.0.21"`, `DEBUG_ENTRIES` still 44 with no ramp knob, and `git diff --name-only` shows only `asteroids-deluxe.html` plus the twelve repointed test files — the GDD, `GDD-VERSION-HISTORY.md` and `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` are untouched (P4's job). Committed per `CLAUDE.md`'s commit-per-phase rule; did **not** push. **Next: the ⛔ PLAYTEST GATE**, then P4.

**Prior session (CS022 P2, landed):** implemented CS022 Phase 2 per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS022.md` Correction C3 and §6/§9, re-grepping every anchor by symbol. Scope was exactly the two functions and the one retirement the phase prompt named — `orbitRadiusStepFor`, `orbitEffectiveCount`, `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP_PAD` — and nothing else. **No genuine design decision surfaced**; the spec's Correction C3 and §6 fully settled both functions' new rules. The full write-up — the math, the measured at-current-geometry consequence, the repoint, and the test counts — is in the rolling header above rather than repeated here, since this phase is small enough that duplicating it would only invite the two copies to drift.

**Prior session (CS022 P1, landed):** implemented CS022 Phase 1 per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS022.md` §4.1/§4.2/§4.3 and FORK-CS022-B/C/D, re-grepping every anchor by symbol (the spec's line numbers are CS021-P4 estimates and had all drifted). Scope was the seven phase-prompt items and nothing else. **No genuine design decision surfaced** — the spec settled every fork this phase touches — but three things are flagged below that the prompt did not predict.

**FIVE EDITS IN `asteroids-deluxe.html`.** **(1) The constants block:** `VIEW_W`/`VIEW_H` move above the world declaration (they are `worldDims`'s input), `WORLD_SIZE_FIELD` 4 / `WORLD_SIZE_ORBIT` 16 / `WORLD_SIZE_MAX` (**derived** as `Math.max` of the two, not restated), `let WORLD_W = 2560, WORLD_H = 1440` — **was `const`** — plus `worldDims(size)` (`sqrt` because a size counts AREA; 4 → 2560×1440 and 16 → 5120×2880, both exact integers) and `worldSizeFor(level)`. **(2) `game.worldSize`**, in BOTH the `game` literal and `startGame()`'s reset, per the standing CS016 P3 rule — and there is exactly ONE writer of it in the whole build (`applyWorldSize`), so the two can never diverge. **(3) `applyWorldSize(size)` + `resizeWorld(newSize)`**, a new block immediately above `startGame()`. **(4) `nextWave()`** gains two lines between the achievement block and `game.dock = new Dock()`. **(5) The starfield**: `stars` laid over `worldDims(WORLD_SIZE_MAX)` once, `starsActive` filtered per world, `drawStarfield`'s far loop repointed onto it. `SPAWN_MAX_DIST`'s comment corrected per spec C5 (the constant did not move). `GAME_VERSION` untouched at `"1.0.0.21"` (TRAP 1); GDD / `GDD-VERSION-HISTORY.md` / `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` untouched (TRAP 2 — verified with `git diff --name-only`); no `ORBIT_*` constant, no `generateOrbitLayout` change, no `levelDef` change, no orbit-spawn change (TRAP 3, pinned positively in the new suite).

**THE ORDERING IS THE PHASE, AND ALL FOUR ORDERING CONSTRAINTS ARE NOW PINNED BEHAVIOURALLY AS WELL AS BY SOURCE REGEX.** (a) In `nextWave()`, the resize runs AFTER `game.wave++` (so `worldSizeFor` reads the new level) and BEFORE `new Dock()` (dock placement is ship-relative and wrap-aware, so it needs both the new period and the re-centred ship) — observed by asserting the new dock lands in `[DOCK_MIN_DIST, DOCK_MAX_DIST]` of the ship and inside the new world, which a resize-after-Dock mutant fails. (b) In `startGame()`, `applyWorldSize(WORLD_SIZE_FIELD)` runs BEFORE `game.ship = new Ship()`, because `Ship.reset()` reads `WORLD_W/2` directly — observed by dying-and-restarting from an orbit level and asserting the ship spawns at (1280, 720) in a 2560×1440 world, not at the stale (2560, 1440). (c) Inside `resizeWorld`, the body AND chain snapshots both use the OLD period and the placement the NEW one, so they straddle the dimension assignment and cannot be reordered around it — a mutant that moves `applyWorldSize` to the top of the function fails 11 assertions. (d) The camera sync is last, so a `draw()` landing between the resize and the next `update()` cannot show a stale view.

**THE CHAIN'S FLOAT CONTRACT, STATED HONESTLY RATHER THAN OVERCLAIMED.** The phase prompt and spec §8 item 5 ask for chain-node `x − px` / `y − py` to be **bit-identical** across a resize. `resizeWorld` shifts `x` and `px` by the *identical double*, which is the correct implementation — but bit-identity is not a property IEEE-754 provides: both additions have to be individually exact, and they are not always. A 2M-sample fuzz over realistic coordinates (positions 0–5120, chain offsets ≤ 480 px, per-frame velocities ≤ 9 px) puts the miss rate near **a third** and the worst deviation at **2.274e-13 px** — one half-ulp at world-coordinate magnitude, i.e. ~1e-11 px/s of implied velocity. The alternative formulation (capture `v`, set `x`, rebuild `px = x − v`) was fuzzed head-to-head and is **exactly as inexact**, so there is no better implementation to switch to. The suite therefore asserts a **hard, documented ceiling of 1e-12 px** on every node in every driven transition, AND proves `===` on a case built from exactly-representable coordinates (where any scaling or clamping of the chain breaks it outright), AND carries a control showing that moving `x` without `px` changes the implied velocity by ~256 px/frame. Asserting `===` universally would have shipped a flaky test.

**BULLETS ARE DELIBERATELY NOT RE-HOMED, and this is the one risk the phase prompt did not flag.** Spec §4.2 enumerates the seven carried arrays and `game.bullets` is not among them; the spec was followed rather than silently widened. Practical effect: a bullet in flight across a transition keeps its old absolute coordinates. On a grow it is simply somewhere else relative to the player and dies on its own life timer; on a shrink `wrap()` folds it on the next frame (one period is always enough here, since 5120 < 2×2560 + 60). Nothing is lost or duplicated and nothing measures from a bullet. Flagged because it is a real, if cosmetic, behaviour and a future phase may want it in the list.

**A MEASURED CONSEQUENCE THAT MATTERS TO CS022 P3's FRAME-BUDGET GATE, found by the regression rather than predicted.** `test-cs021-p1.js` §K's level-3 orbit probe is unchanged code, but its numbers moved because the level now runs in a 4× world: **peak standing garbage 256 → 319** and **median `update(dt)` 0.278 ms → 0.584 ms** (p99 0.907 → 1.213), against an unchanged level-4 field control. The mechanism is coalescence, not the resize: the same garbage spread over four times the area is farther apart, so `coalesceGarbage` merges less of it and more pieces stand. **A bigger world makes the standing-garbage load HIGHER, not lower** — the opposite of the intuition that more space means more room — and spec §8 item 9's ceiling must be derived against the size-16 world, not extrapolated from CS021's size-4 measurement.

**ONE TEST IN THE EXISTING SUITE HAD GONE VACUOUS RATHER THAN MERELY STALE, WHICH IS WHY THE SWEEP WAS RUN WIDE.** `test-cs021-p1.js` §H's end-to-end case parked the ship at `(2, WORLD_H − 3)` and drove level 2 → 3 to force a seam-side dock; under CS022 that transition resizes and `resizeWorld` re-centres the ship, so the dock was placed nowhere near a seam and the check passed for the wrong reason. Repointed to use the mechanism rather than fight it: reach level 3 FIRST (that transition does the growing), then place the ship on the LIVE world's seam, then drive **3 → 6** — both orbit levels, so `worldSizeFor` is unchanged, no resize runs, and the ship stays put. A naive-arithmetic control (off by 2,666 px) was added so it can never go quiet again. **The general lesson, worth carrying into P2/P3: every test's `WORLD_W`/`WORLD_H` is a destructured LOAD-TIME SNAPSHOT of the FIELD size; anything needing the live period must read `worldDims(game.worldSize)`.**

**REPOINT SWEEP — ELEVEN FILES, AND THE PROMPT'S THREE-FILE LIST WAS RIGHT AS FAR AS IT WENT.** Six files failed outright and five more were reviewed and either fixed or explicitly guarded. **Failed and fixed:** `test-v31-world.js` (§A's literals restated as `worldDims(WORLD_SIZE_FIELD)` + the orbit-size limit; §B's wrap-clean budget read live per trial; §D's area derivation moved to `WORLD_SIZE_MAX`), `test-starfield.js` (§D's probe star taken from `starsActive`, since `stars[0]` may now lie outside the live board — plus four new subset assertions), `test-cs017-p5.js` (the bonus-canister world-bounds check now reads the live period; its sweep walks orbit levels), `test-cs018-p1.js` (§I required every `levelDef(` call site to read `game.wave`; `worldSizeFor(level)` is a parameterised reader, so the exemption is **narrow, named, and paid for** by two new assertions that every CALL of it passes `game.wave` and that no other parameterised reader exists), `test-cs021-p1.js` (§A's "exactly one archetype consumer" is now two, both named positively rather than loosened to a count; §H above; §I's random ship placement now samples the live world instead of one quadrant; §K's death-shockwave variant no longer relies on ambient contact damage killing a parked ship — in a 5120×2880 world drifting debris no longer wraps back around within the probe window, so the variant had silently degenerated into its own control, and it now calls the real `killShip()` on a fixed frame), `test-cs021-p1b.js` (§I/§J stage on an orbit level and place things at "the seam" — read live; `worldDims` went on the FIXED-build-only list because `RETURN` is shared with a `PRE_FIX_REF` build that predates the seam). **Passing but silently measuring the wrong thing, fixed anyway:** `test-cs021-p2.js` §D budgeted orbit geometry against `WORLD_H/2 − 20` = 700 from the field-size snapshot; it is now `worldDims(WORLD_SIZE_ORBIT)`-derived = 1420. **This one would have actively mis-passed at P3**, whose 1,334 px outer edge fails against 700. **Reviewed and guarded rather than changed:** `test-cs020-p1.js` / `test-cs020-p1b.js` park the dock at the snapshot's centre and reach level 12 (an orbit level) — repointing to the live centre was tried and **reverted**, because §J compares floater world coordinates byte-for-byte against a `PRE_FIX_REF` build that has no world-size seam, so moving only the fixed build breaks a cross-build identity that is about the MECHANIC; the placement is fine (1280 px from the nearest seam in either world) and now carries a hard guard that throws if it ever isn't. `test-f5.js` §H2's seam case was correct only because §D's last real `nextWave()` happens to land on level 200, a field level — that luck is now an assertion. **Reviewed and left alone, with the reason:** the other eighteen `WORLD_*` files never drive a real `nextWave()` past a field level (most never call it at all), and `test-cs015-p3.js` §C is self-guarding — its `ship.x === WORLD_W/2` assertion after `startGame()` IS the check that the world was reset.

**New `scratchpad/test-cs022-p1.js` (178 assertions, sections A–I, 0 failures, byte-identical across three consecutive runs)** drives the REAL `startGame`/`nextWave`/`update(1/60)`/`draw`/`resizeWorld`/`applyWorldSize`/`drawStarfield` — nothing under test is reimplemented. Every `build()` runs inside a seeded LCG **because the starfield is laid down with `Math.random()` at module load**, without which §H's figures would differ run to run and this file could never be byte-identical in a sweep. **(A)** `node --check` plus source pins: the `let` declaration and that nothing re-declares it `const`, the derived `WORLD_SIZE_MAX`, the both-places `game.worldSize` rule and its single writer, all four orderings by source index, `dmax` on the NEW dimensions, the chain's identical-delta shift and that it reaches for neither `dmax` nor a scale factor, one definition and exactly two/one call sites for `applyWorldSize`/`resizeWorld`, the starfield seam, and all three TRAPs — including that the near parallax block contains no `WORLD_` reference at all. **(B)** spec §8 item 4: `worldSizeFor` agrees with `levelDef().archetype` at every level 1–200 (66 orbit / 134 field), then levels 2–31 driven for real with the LIVE dimensions checked after every single `nextWave()` — 10 orbit levels exactly 5120×2880, 20 field levels exactly 2560×1440, 10 grows and 10 shrinks — and the round trip lands on **exactly** 2560/1440 with `===`. Plus the restart-from-an-orbit-level case, and proof that field→field and orbit→orbit boundaries resize nothing and move the ship not at all. **(C)** spec §8 item 5 in both directions with 140 staged bodies across all seven arrays: none dropped, ship exactly centred, camera synced, **worst bearing error 4.7e-15 rad**, worst distance error under 1e-6 px against `min(old, dmax)`, nothing outside the new world, and the shrink genuinely clamping a band while leaving the near ones alone. **(D)** the rejected FORK-CS022-D option (a) as a live control: a bare `wrap()` re-home swings bearings by up to **3.124 rad** and teleports **88 of 120** bodies by more than 200 px. **(E)** the chain, per the paragraph above. **(F)** the same claims with the ship hard into a world corner in both directions, with a straddler count as its own control. **(G)** 640 garbage bodies through a real `resizeWorld` shrink: array length unchanged, every entry a distinct object, all 640 identity tags intact, every one inside the new world, and **FLAG-CS022-k observed** — 341 of 640 parked in a shell at exactly `dmax` (660 px). **(H)** spec §8 item 6: `STAR_COUNT` 1280 area-derived for `WORLD_SIZE_MAX`, the active count matching the area-derived 320 at field size within 5σ of the binomial sample (banded because a uniform subset IS a sample, not because the number wanders), the field sky a **strict real transitions with `AudioSys` present and absent. **Mutation-tested against fourteen deliberate breakages**, every one caught: chain `px` not shifted (4 failures), chain scaled like a body (3), **BASELINE MEASURED BEFORE EDITING: 90 files, 0 failures, every exit code 0** (9,971 assertions across the files using the `"N passed, M failed"` format, plus 7 files on the `RESULT: PASS` format), captured as full per-file stdout+exit-code snapshots. **Full regression after, run twice consecutively: 91 files (the new P1 test added), 0 failures and 0 non-zero exits on both runs, 10,169 assertions both times.** Run-to-run: **5 files differ, all of them the already-documented unpinned-`Math.random()`/wall-clock noise** (`test-cs018-p4`, `test-cs021-p1`'s frame-budget probe, `test-v31-world`, `test-v33-p2`, `test-v33-p3`) — no new nondeterminism, and the new file is byte-identical. Baseline-to-after: 9 files differ — those 5, plus exactly the 4 whose assertion text or count this session changed (`test-cs018-p1` +3, `test-cs021-p2`'s reported budget 700 → 1420, `test-f5` +1, `test-starfield` +4), each diffed line by line. The other repointed files (`test-cs017-p5`, `test-cs020-p1`, `test-cs020-p1b`, `test-cs021-p1b`) produce byte-identical output to their baseline, which is the point: their claims did not change, only what they read them from. Committed per `CLAUDE.md`'s commit-per-phase rule; did **not** push. **Next: CS022 P2** — `orbitRadiusStepFor` holds the step fixed and `orbitEffectiveCount` clamps on the wrap-clean budget. Note for that session: it must read `worldDims(WORLD_SIZE_ORBIT)` and **not** the live `WORLD_H`, which is 1440 whenever a field level is on screen; `test-cs021-p2.js` §D was repointed this session onto exactly that idiom and is the pattern to copy.

**Prior session (CS021 P5, landed — CS021 COMPLETE):** implemented CS021 Phase 5 per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS021.md` P5 — the closing phase: retune from the gate, version bump, doc sweep. **No new logic, and — unusually — no retune either.** Archive check (item 5) verified first and passed: `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS020.md` and `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS020.md` were already in `archive/` from Paul's own setup step (commit `4119c75`); nothing moved.

**THE GATE ANSWER IS A FEATURE, NOT A RETUNE, AND IT WAS SURFACED RATHER THAN INTERPRETED.** Gate Q1 came back: *the orbits are too close to the dock and all of them are too tight — first orbit the equivalent of 5 large satellites out from the dock, each subsequent orbit 3 more out from the one inside it*, with Paul noting the level may need to be larger to accommodate them and pointing forward at the per-level world-size scheme (size 1 / 4 / 9 / 16 screens) he wants in a later changeset. Items 2–8 were explicitly unanswerable — *"could not be tested practically, because of the orbits being too tight."* The arithmetic, run before touching anything, is why this could not be a P5 retune. Large satellite diameter is 92 px (`DEBRIS_RADII[3] = 46`), so the request is `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS` **180 → 460** and `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` **150 → 276**, putting four rings at **460 / 736 / 1012 / 1288** and the outermost satellite EDGE at **1,334 px**. This world's largest wrap-clean circle is **700 px** (`WORLD_H/2 − 20`, pinned by three test files). **Ring 2's edge is 782 px — only ONE ring fits.** Nor is there a useful middle ground: holding the same 5:3 ratio and filling the 700 budget gives 4 rings at inner 234 / step 140, barely distinguishable from the shipped 180/150. Fitting the request needs `min(WORLD_W, WORLD_H) ≥ 2708`; against Paul's own future size scheme (`VIEW` is 1280×720, so the current 2560×1440 world already IS "size 4"), **size 9 (3840×2160) fits only 3 rings by a 2 px margin, and 4 rings needs size 16 (5120×2880)**. That is a ~2× world across 27 `WORLD_*` references, un-gated, in a phase whose prompt says "no new logic" — and it is precisely the feature Paul had already scheduled for later. Per `CLAUDE.md`'s stop-and-surface rule the decision went back to Paul rather than being picked: three options (close CS021 now with the geometry to CS022 / hold CS021 open with no version bump until a second gate / grow the world inside P5). **Paul chose: close CS021 now, geometry to CS022.** So nothing was retuned — "no change" answers move nothing, and the one non-"no change" answer moves to CS022.

**ONE CONSEQUENCE OF Q1 THAT IS NOT ITSELF A GATE ANSWER, AND WHICH CS022 MUST DECIDE BEFORE IT BUILDS ANYTHING.** `maxCount` scales with circumference, so at Q1's radii and the *shipped* density curve the satellite count goes **40 → 87** size-3s (rings of 14/14/15/44 at the level-3 multiplier) — more than double, against a normal level's maximum of 13. Paul asked for more *space*, not more *satellites*. That reopens FORK-CS021-D (the accepted bonanza) and FLAG-CS021-h (the untested frame budget) together, and the density curve is the dial — but which way it moves is a design answer nobody has given yet. **CS022 should settle world size, ring geometry AND the density curve as one decision, then run the second gate iteration Paul asked for.**

**VERSION BUMP: ELEVEN PINS, NOT THE EIGHT-PLUS-FOUR THE PHASE DOC PREDICTED.** `GAME_VERSION` `"1.0.0.20"` → **`"1.0.0.21"`** in `asteroids-deluxe.html`, and the repo was grepped whole rather than trusted to any list — which was right again. **Six live pins that track HEAD**, each with both its console label and its assert message bumped: `test-cs010-p0.js` (four sites, including a fresh `HighScores.add()` record's `build` stamp — the one pin that is not just a `GAME_VERSION` comparison), `test-cs013-p4.js`, `test-cs015-p7.js`, `test-cs016-p5.js`, `test-cs017-p7.js`, `test-cs018-p10.js`. **Five CS021 files, not four**, repointed from their own "unchanged this phase" pins to the mirror image (`assert GAME_VERSION !== "1.0.0.20"`, the standing treatment — `test-cs021-p1.js`'s own comment had predicted this exact repoint): `test-cs021-p1.js`, **`test-cs021-p1b.js`** (the file the phase doc's "four new CS021 test files" count missed, since P1b was an in-round corrective phase never written into the phases doc), `test-cs021-p2.js`, `test-cs021-p3.js` (TWO sites — §A and §L), `test-cs021-p4.js`. Each mirror-image pin switched from `eq(...)` to `assert(... !== ...)` and carries a `REPOINTED BY CS021 P5` note. Historical header-comment narratives left alone per the CS018 P10 / CS020 P2 precedent.

**Prior session (CS021 P3, landed):** implemented CS021 Phase 3 per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS021.md` §6 and FLAG-CS021-a/b/c/g, `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS021.md` P3, re-grepping every P1/P2 anchor by symbol (P2 moved `spawnOrbitWave`'s signature and placed `orbitGapMult` beside the other orbit pure helpers, exactly as P2's own STATUS note predicted). **SEVEN EDITS, ALL IN `asteroids-deluxe.html`.** **(1) `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP_PAD = 40`**, beside the other `ORBIT_*` constants — the padding FLAG-CS021-a adds to the (call-site-derived) satellite diameter to floor an auto-derived `radiusStep`. **(2) The ten-entry `ORBIT` section in `DEBUG_VARS`**, ninth header, registry 34 → 44: `orbitGapMult` (1.5–4.0, step 0.05, def `ORBIT_GAP_MULT`), `orbitSafetyMargin` (0–32 px, def `ORBIT_SAFETY_MARGIN`), `orbitCount` (3–5, def `ORBIT_RING_COUNT`, carries `clampShown` — see below), `orbitDensity1`–`orbitDensity5` (0–1, defs `ORBIT_DENSITY[0..3]`; `orbitDensity5` has no shipped 5th-ring value to derive from, so it mirrors ring 4's climax density, `ORBIT_DENSITY[3]`, rather than inventing a number), `orbitAngVel` (°/s, def `ORBIT_ANG_VEL * 180/Math.PI` = 6, `toNative: v => v * Math.PI/180`, the `unit:"ms"` precedent's degrees/radians sibling), `orbitFastMult` (1.0–6.0×, def `ORBIT_FAST_MULT`). **(3) `applyDebug()` gains `clampShown`** — the one new registry hook this phase adds, applied generically (`const clamped = e.clampShown ? e.clampShown(shown) : shown;`) before either map is written, so all 43 entries without one are byte-unchanged and only `orbitCount` is affected. **(4) `orbitRadiusStepFor(count)` + `orbitEffectiveCount(requested)`** — the geometry clamp (FLAG-CS021-a). **(5) `orbitEffectiveGapMult(level)`** — the debug override (spec §6 table: "overrides ... while ≠ def"). **(6) `spawnOrbitWave()`** now reads `DEBUG.orbitSafetyMargin`/`orbitDensity1-5`/`orbitAngVel`/`orbitFastMult`/`orbitCount` directly and derives `radiusStep` from the (already-clamped) `orbitCount` via `orbitRadiusStepFor`; `gapMult` stays a caller-supplied parameter (P2's seam), now fed `orbitEffectiveGapMult(game.wave)` from `nextWave()`. **(7) `rerollOrbitStartAngles()`** plus a raw `"r"` keydown-listener branch and a `game.orbitLayout` field (declared in the game literal beside `dock: null`, assigned by BOTH `nextWave()` branches — the spawn's return on orbit levels, `null` on field levels, same unconditional-every-call treatment as `dock`). `GAME_VERSION` untouched at `"1.0.0.20"` (TRAP 1); GDD / `GDD-VERSION-HISTORY.md` / `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` untouched (TRAP 2 — P5's job).

**THE GEOMETRY CLAMP HOLDS THE OUTER EDGE FIXED, NOT THE STEP, AND THAT CHOICE IS LOAD-BEARING.** `orbitRadiusStepFor(count)` redistributes the SAME outer radius the shipped 4-ring geometry reaches (`ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS + (ORBIT_RING_COUNT − 1) × ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` = 630, fixed regardless of the debug knob) evenly across `count − 1` gaps, rather than deriving from the ~700 px wrap-clean budget directly. At the shipped default (`count === 4`) this reproduces `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` (150) **bit-for-bit** — verified, not assumed — which is what keeps P1/P2's pinned geometry table (counts 6/6/7/21, outer edge 676) from moving a pixel unless the knob is actually touched. At `count 3` the step widens to 225; at a requested `count 5` it would narrow to 112.5, below the 132 px floor (`DEBRIS_RADII[3]*2 + ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP_PAD`), which is exactly what `orbitEffectiveCount` walks back down from 5 to 4.

**ONE GENUINE DESIGN DECISION SURFACED, RESOLVED FROM THE SPEC'S OWN WORDING RATHER THAN INVENTED: WHAT "THE PANEL SHOWS THE CLAMPED VALUE" ACTUALLY REQUIRES.** The spec (FLAG-CS021-a) and the phase prompt both say a requested `orbitCount` of 5 "clamps down" and "the panel shows the clamped value" — but `toNative` (the registry's one existing extension point) only transforms display→native for the CONSUMER; it can't make the PANEL itself show something other than what was typed without either bespoke render code for one row (breaking "standard registry idiom") or a second registry hook. I added exactly one: `clampShown`, applied inside `applyDebug()` itself, so `debugShown.orbitCount` — what's rendered AND what's persisted — is never anything other than the achievable value; requesting 5 round-trips straight back to 4 everywhere at once (shown, native, saved), never a slider that visibly says one number while a different one spawns. This is the smallest addition that satisfies the literal requirement without touching `drawDebug()`, and it costs nothing for the other 43 entries (`e.clampShown` is `undefined`, so `shown` passes through unchanged — proved in `test-cs021-p3.js` §A/§B). Flagging it here because it IS a new mechanism, even though a narrow, data-only one.

**THE REROLL TOUCHES START ANGLES ONLY, LITERALLY** — read per the phase prompt's item 4 rather than the spec §6 closing paragraph's looser "changed values take effect ... immediately via reroll," which is true only in the sense that reroll is the sole way to see ANY interactive feedback on the CURRENT wave (a slider drag alone never mid-wave-regenerates); it does not re-read any debug knob. `rerollOrbitStartAngles()` calls `placeOrbitRing` (P1's own helper, which touches nothing but `startAngle`/`satellites`) on every ring, re-runs `spawnSafeOrbitLayout` (the identical pass a fresh spawn gets), then re-derives every LIVE entity's `x`/`y`/`orbitAngle` by matching it back to its ring **by `orbitRadius`** — the ring-identity field every existing orbit test already keys off — in surviving creation order, rather than tagging entities with a new field. A ring that lost members to harvesting simply has fewer survivors to reposition; nothing crashes, nothing duplicates. Verified against a partial-harvest case (two of ring 1's six satellites destroyed before reroll) in `test-cs021-p3.js` §I.

**THE REPOINT SURFACE WAS FAR WIDER THAN THE FOUR FILES THE PHASE PROMPT NAMED — TEN FILES TOTAL, FOUND BY RUNNING THE WHOLE SUITE RATHER THAN TRUSTING THE LIST.** The prompt named `test-cs018-p4.js` §H, `test-cs018-p6.js` §I, `test-cs018-p7.js` §H, `test-cs020-p1.js` TRAP 3 — all four repointed (34 → 44, plus a positive assertion that the ten new entries are CS021 P3's ORBIT knobs). **Six more surfaced on the sweep, exactly as CS020 P1b's lesson predicted it would again:** **`test-cs020-p1b.js`** (its own "33 → 34, P1b adds exactly one knob" pin, same treatment). **`test-cs021-p1.js`** §A — TWO assertions, not one: the bare count AND "no ORBIT knob has been added ahead of P3," which P3 satisfies by existing, so it flips to a positive "exactly the ten expected ids are present" check. **`test-cs021-p1b.js`** §A — the same bare-count TRAP 3. **`test-cs021-p2.js`** §A — not a count pin at all but a literal source-regex pinning the OLD call site text `spawnOrbitWave(speedMul, orbitGapMult(game.wave))`, which P3 replaced with `orbitEffectiveGapMult(game.wave)`; repointed to the new text plus a new assertion that `orbitEffectiveGapMult` is defined exactly once. **And two CROSS-CHANGESET GENERIC whole-registry regression tests that don't pin the count at all** — `test-cs015-p5.js` §D (steps every non-excluded entry to its declared `max` via real `menuDebug` presses and asserts it lands there) and `test-cs018-p2.js` §G (types a computed in-range value into every entry and asserts the exact round-trip) — both failed specifically and only on `orbitCount`, the one entry whose `clampShown` makes its stored value diverge from its own declared `max` / from a naively-clamped typed value. Both repointed to account for `clampShown` generically rather than weakening the claim for the other 43–53 entries: `test-cs015-p5.js` now excludes `orbitCount` from the sweep (with a note explaining why, mirroring its existing `autoShieldRegenPause` exclusion); `test-cs018-p2.js` now runs the expected value through `e.clampShown` too, so `orbitCount` stays IN the sweep and is proven to commit to its clamped value rather than being skipped.

**Prior session (CS021 P2, landed):** implemented CS021 Phase 2 per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS021.md` §5 and `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS021.md` P2, re-grepping every P1 anchor by symbol since P1b moved them. **THREE EDITS, ALL IN `asteroids-deluxe.html`.** **(1) Two constants** beside `ORBIT_GAP_MULT`: `ORBIT_GAP_MULT_FLOOR` **1.8** and `ORBIT_GAP_MULT_STEP` **0.1** — `ORBIT_GAP_MULT` itself is unchanged at 2.5 and is now read as "the occurrence-1 base value," not "the multiplier." **(2) `orbitGapMult(level)`**, a pure function (`occurrence = level / ORBIT_LEVEL_EVERY; return Math.max(ORBIT_GAP_MULT_FLOOR, ORBIT_GAP_MULT - (occurrence - 1) * ORBIT_GAP_MULT_STEP)`), placed beside `orbitTangent`/`orbitSyncVelocity` right before `spawnOrbitWave()` — P1's own STATUS note ("do not push a constant read down into the generator; that is the seam both later phases turn on") is exactly why: `generateOrbitLayout()` itself is byte-unchanged and still has no idea occurrence exists. **(3) `spawnOrbitWave(speedMul, gapMult)`** gained a second parameter, feeding it to `minGapMultiplier` in place of the literal `ORBIT_GAP_MULT`; `nextWave()`'s orbit branch now calls `spawnOrbitWave(speedMul, orbitGapMult(game.wave))`. `GAME_VERSION` untouched at `"1.0.0.20"` (TRAP 1); GDD / `GDD-VERSION-HISTORY.md` untouched (P5's job).

**THE ARITHMETIC REPRODUCES SPEC §5 TO THE DIGIT:** level 3 (occurrence 1) → 2.5×; level 21 (occurrence 7) → 1.9×, one step short; level 24 (occurrence 8) → **1.8×, the first floor level**; level 63 (occurrence 21) → still 1.8×, held. Widening `maxCount` as the multiplier tightens steps the total up non-monotonically-in-appearance-but-actually-monotonic: 40 (occ 1–2) → 41 (occ 3, level 9) → 42 (occ 4, level 12) → 44 (occ 5–7, levels 15–21) → **45 (occ 8+, level 24 on)**, maxCounts widening ring-by-ring from 7/13/19/25 to 8/14/21/28, per-ring counts at the floor landing on 6/7/8/24 — all pinned exactly against the real generator in `test-cs021-p2.js` §C, both via the pure formula and via a REAL `nextWave()` spawn grouped by `orbitRadius`.

**`DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` §3 gains one row (FLAG-CS021-d), grepped to the existing table format and inserted without restructuring anything else:** "Orbit gap multiplier" — constants, the occurrence-scaled mechanism (a genuinely new one, distinct from `leverScale`/`ramp()`/the level table proper, though still ultimately a function of `game.wave`), the 2.5×→1.8× curve, and a note that `ORBIT_DENSITY` and both angular velocities stay fixed (the spec's own "change one variable at a time" rule).

**THE REPOINT SURFACE WAS WIDER THAN THE PHASE PROMPT NAMED, DISCOVERED BY RUNNING THE SUITE RATHER THAN GUESSING.** The prompt's own P1 test file (`test-cs021-p1.js` §C) had already flagged the risk in a comment ("P1 ships ONE gap multiplier for every occurrence; P2 is what makes it scale"), but five files actually hardcoded "an orbit level spawns exactly 40" as a REAL-`nextWave()`-driven assertion, not just the generator-level formula check §C itself makes (which stayed correct unchanged, since it always feeds the FIXED base constant, not `orbitGapMult`): **`test-cs017-p1.js`** (§B, §F — real per-level sweeps to 28), **`test-cs017-p2.js`** (§B — the `DiffLog` integration sweep), **`test-cs017-p3.js`** (§B, §B4b, §G — sweeps to 63, 130, and probes at 66/99/909/2000 for the endgame plateau), **`test-cs018-p3.js`** (§B — the 1–63 debris-count integration sweep), and **`test-cs021-p1.js`** itself (§D — the field-levels-untouched sweep, which also exercises every orbit level as its mirror check). Every one now computes its expectation from the SAME `generateOrbitLayout()` + `orbitGapMult()` the shipped code is wired to (a wiring check, not a restated literal) via a small `orbitTotalAt(A, level)` helper added to each file (four of the five needed `orbitGapMult`/`generateOrbitLayout`/the `ORBIT_*` constants added to their `RETURN` arrays, none of which pre-dated CS021 in those older CS017/CS018 files). **Zero of the five needed their actual test LOGIC changed** — only the expected-value literal became a computed one; every surrounding assertion (archetype classification, junk-cycle shape, DiffLog field agreement, endgame plateau) is unchanged and still passes.

**A GEOMETRIC FINDING, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED, THAT CHANGED HOW THE MUTANT TEST IS SHAPED.** The phase prompt states as fact that "with the 1.8 clamp removed, some deep level MUST violate the floor." My first draft tried to prove that as a GEOMETRIC claim — feed the real, unmodified `generateOrbitLayout()` an unclamped (occurrence-driven, arbitrarily low or negative) multiplier and check whether some ring's `actualGapPx` drops under the true 46.8 px floor (26 × 1.8). **It does not, at the shipped `ORBIT_DENSITY` curve** (max 0.85, never 1.0): `generateOrbitLayout`'s own ADDITIVE floor (`shipDiameter + ORBIT_SAFETY_MARGIN` = 34 px) caps how tight `minRequiredGap` can ever get regardless of how low the multiplier goes, and at that additive floor the rounded `count` still lands enough below `maxCount` at every ring (even ring 4's 0.85 density) that the worst measured arc gap was **54.6 px — comfortably above 46.8**, however far below 1.8 the fed-in multiplier was pushed (even negative). So the clamp is not protecting the GEOMETRY at these densities — it is protecting the REPORTED VALUE from being a nonsensical, misleading number. The mutant check (`test-cs021-p2.js` §E) was reshaped to test exactly that: the unclamped closed-form formula (no `Math.max`) genuinely reports a multiplier below 1.8 starting at occurrence 9 (level 27, well inside the shipped 3–63 range) and goes negative past level 81, while the REAL `orbitGapMult()` never does, at any of the 21 orbit levels — real teeth, just at the value level rather than the geometry. **Flagging this for the gate rather than treating it as settled:** if a future density retune ever pushes any ring's density toward 1.0, the geometric floor could start binding for real, at which point this finding stops being academic.

**A SECOND OBSERVATION, INVESTIGATED RATHER THAN LEFT AS A LOOSE THREAD.** Re-running `test-cs017-p3.js` (which drives real `nextWave()` sweeps under a CONSTANT-valued `Math.random()` stub, `PIN = 0.5`, for reproducibility) produced repeated `console.warn("orbit spawn safety: ring 4 exhausted 60 rerolls...")` lines that were not there pre-P2. Investigated with a throwaway probe (outside the repo, deleted before commit — the CS020 TRAP-4 precedent) rather than assumed benign: under `PIN = 0.5`, `rand(0, angleStep)` returns the IDENTICAL value on every reroll attempt, so if the very first placement is unsafe, every one of the 60 reroll attempts reproduces that exact same unsafe placement — a property of the constant-RNG test harness, not of real gameplay, and it surfaces more often post-P2 simply because deeper occurrences pack more satellites into ring 4 (narrower `angleStep`, so a fixed relative ship position is statistically more likely to land within `minRequiredGap` of one of them on the first, only-ever-tried angle). **A genuinely-random probe (200 real wave starts each, `Math.random()` un-pinned) at levels 3/24/30/63/99/300 found spawn safety holds fine in practice**: worst ship-to-satellite clearance measured 65.64 px at level 3 (floor 65) and 47.15–49.45 px at levels 24 and beyond (floor 46.8) — consistent with P1's own measured 66.32 px at level 3. No code change made; this is a test-harness artifact, not a regression, and it's recorded here because gate question 6 ("any wave start where the ship materialised inside or dangerously near a ring?") is exactly what it touches.

**New `scratchpad/test-cs021-p2.js` (622 assertions, sections A–E, 0 failures, deterministic — byte-identical across two consecutive runs, the one file in this session's suite with zero RNG-driven console noise).** **(A)** `node --check` + source pins: TRAP 1, exactly one `orbitGapMult` definition and its exact clamp/decay expression, the two new constants each declared once at spec's values, `generateOrbitLayout()` provably does NOT read `orbitGapMult` (the purity boundary), `spawnOrbitWave`'s signature and its `minGapMultiplier: gapMult` line, the old `minGapMultiplier: ORBIT_GAP_MULT` literal provably GONE, and exactly one call site. **(B)** `gapMult` pinned at levels 3/21/24/63 plus the full decay shape across all 21 occurrences (non-increasing, matches the closed form, never below the floor, holds past the endgame plateau to level 6009) and the "one variable scales" invariant (density curve and the SET of real per-ring angular velocities identical at level 3 vs level 24). **(C)** totals 40/45 and maxCount 7/13/19/25→8/14/21/28 through both the pure generator AND a real `nextWave()` spawn grouped by `orbitRadius` (6/7/8/24), plus a monotonic non-decreasing climb check across all 21 occurrences. **(D)** the full spec §8 fairness sweep re-run at occurrence-scaled values across all 21 orbit levels — item 1 as the TWO-PART claim it actually is (the value-level floor AND the generator's self-consistency, which together transitively guarantee the true 46.8 px geometric floor), items 2–4, plus the same sweep re-run at the P3 debug range's extremes (1.5×, 4.0×) so it doesn't silently assume the shipped curve stays inside [1.8, 2.5]. **(E)** the mutant check, reshaped per the finding above: the unclamped closed-form formula demonstrably violates the 1.8 floor at real in-range levels (level 27 on) while the real, clamped `orbitGapMult()` never does, at any of the 21 levels swept.

**Prior session (CS021 P1b, landed):** an in-round corrective phase (the CS020 P1b precedent), opened by Paul in conversation after reading P1's write-up: *"I would imagine a solution to this is to make the player ship — when shielded — bounce off of the orbiting satellite. Is that what you did? And/or can you make that happen?"* No, it wasn't — and yes. **Scope was agreed before any edit**, per `CLAUDE.md`'s stop-and-surface rule, since none of this is in `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS021.md`: Paul chose *land it now as its own phase* and *elastic — reflect incoming speed*.

**THE DEFECT WAS REPRODUCED AND QUANTIFIED BEFORE ANY EDIT**, by a throwaway probe (deleted before commit, outside the repo entirely — the CS020 TRAP-4 precedent) holding the shield down with no thrust against a single hazard for 10 s. **`shieldDeflect()` shoves the HAZARD and never touches the ship**, which separates every free-moving body — but a CS021 orbiting satellite has its position re-derived from its orbit angle every frame, so the shove is undone before it can separate and the identical contact re-presents on the next frame:

| placement | deflect frames | shield emptied | hull |
|---|---|---|---|
| ORBIT ring 1 (slow), ship at 68 px — the shield-only annulus | **5** | **0.10 s** | 250/250 |
| ORBIT ring 1, ship at 50 px | **5** | **0.10 s** | **200/250, one 50-dmg hit** |
| ORBIT ring 3 (fast), ship at 68 px | **5** | **0.10 s** | 250/250 |
| FIELD control, ordinary drifting size-3 | 1 | 1.38 s | 250/250 |

**A 14× drain — the whole meter gone in a tenth of a second.** **It was NOT a damage bug, which is worth recording because my P1 write-up implied otherwise:** once the shield collapses the ship's hitbox shrinks from `SHIELD_RADIUS` 26 to `SHIP_RADIUS` 13, so anywhere in the 59–72 px annulus the ship simply stops touching and takes nothing. Only inside 59 px does it take a hit — exactly one, after which the existing `KNOCKBACK_SPEED` shove throws it clear (measured: ended 731 px away). So the real failure was a **silent, instantaneous, total loss of a resource**, with no feedback but five `shieldPing`s in 83 ms.

**THREE EDITS, ALL IN `asteroids-deluxe.html`.** **(1) Two constants** beside the other `SHIELD_*` values: `SHIELD_BOUNCE_RESTITUTION` **1.0** (Paul's pick — literally "reflect incoming speed"; the PLAYTEST KNOB to lower if a ring reads like a trampoline) and `SHIELD_BOUNCE_MIN` **120** px/s, the separation floor. **(2) `shieldBounce(obj)`**, `shieldDeflect`'s mirror image, right beside it: everything `shieldDeflect` does to the hazard, this does to the SHIP — separate to `SHIELD_RADIUS + radius + 2`, take the velocity change, pay the same `SHIELD_HIT_COST`, count the same `deflects++`, play the same ping — and **the hazard is left completely untouched**, so the ring stays intact (knocking a satellite off its rail would be a different game; FORK-CS021-C2 keeps rings whole). **(3) One call-site branch**, `} else if (h.orbitCenter) { shieldBounce(h); }`, inserted between the homing-Hunter branch and the existing `else shieldDeflect(h)`. **The gate is the same field the motion mode itself gates on — one concept, not two — and `shieldDeflect` is byte-untouched.** No `game` field, no `DEBUG_VARS` entry, no `localStorage` key; `GAME_VERSION` untouched at `"1.0.0.20"` (TRAP 1); GDD / `GDD-VERSION-HISTORY.md` / `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` untouched (TRAP 2).

**THE BOUNCE IS ELASTIC AND COMPUTED IN THE HAZARD'S OWN FRAME**, which is the part that carries the feel: subtract the satellite's tangential velocity, reflect the **approaching** component of what is left (a receding ship is never yanked backwards), add the satellite's velocity back. The consequence, and it is the classic elastic result against an infinite mass: **a satellite that sweeps into a parked ship shoves it at twice its own speed** — **301.6 px/s off ring 3** (2 × 150.8) against **120 px/s off ring 1** (whose 2 × 18.8 = 37.6 is below the floor, so the floor governs there). That is exactly the "ring 3 trades spatial tightness for timing pressure" story from FORK-CS021-C1, now expressed in the collision as well as in the spacing. A purely tangential approach — riding alongside — has no normal component and correctly does nothing but the floor.

**POST-FIX, same probe:** every orbit placement is **1 deflect frame / 1.38 s of shield / 250 hull, byte-identical to the FIELD control**, and the 50 px case's 50-damage hit is **gone** (the bounce separates before the shield can collapse). The field control itself is unchanged on both builds, which is the point: this phase changes nothing for a hazard that can be pushed.

**I WAS WRONG ABOUT WHY THE FLOOR MATTERS, AND MEASURED IT RATHER THAN LEAVING THE CLAIM STANDING.** The first draft of `SHIELD_BOUNCE_MIN`'s comment called it load-bearing for "a stationary ship in the annulus" — but the mutation sweep showed the suite couldn't catch the floor being removed, and a direct A/B probe explained why: the overlap push alone buys 2 px of clearance, which is enough for a ship parked *radially* beside a slow satellite. The floor earns its place in the cases a player **creates on purpose** (deflect events over 5 s, floor 120 vs floor 0): parked in the satellite's **path** 1 vs 2; **thrust held** into the ring **2 vs 5**; arriving with a **12-node tow** whose `CARGO_TUG` drags them back in, 1 vs 2. The constant's comment now states those measured numbers, and §D asserts all three behaviourally.

**BASELINE: 86 files, 0 failures** (the P1 commit's own figure). **Full regression after, run twice consecutively: 87 files, 0 failures on both runs** (35,993 / 35,995 assertions — the only per-file difference is `test-cs018-p4.js`'s documented RNG-dependent assertion count). **ZERO existing test files needed repointing** — the whole change is additive behind a branch no pre-CS021 hazard can reach, and the per-file diff against the P1 baseline is exactly one new file.

**New `scratchpad/test-cs021-p1b.js` (257 assertions, sections (A)–(K), 0 failures, deterministic across repeated runs)** drives the REAL `startGame`/`update(1/60)`/collision path; **(B)** additionally builds the pre-fix module from the **fixed SHA `1aba2c2`** (CS021 P1 — never `HEAD`, the trap `test-cs017-p3.js` fell into) as a permanent red control, so "5 deflects / 0.10 s" is pinned against the actual previous build rather than a restated expectation. **(A)** `node --check` + source pins: TRAP 1, TRAP 3, exactly one `shieldBounce` definition and exactly one call site, the `else if (h.orbitCenter)` gate with `shieldDeflect` still the else, the homing-Hunter branch still first, **`shieldDeflect`'s body proved to still contain no `game.ship.x/y/vx/vy` assignment** and `shieldBounce`'s proved to contain no assignment to the hazard at all, `angleTo` and `wrap` used, both constants named and declared once. **(C)** the elastic invariants over 40 incoming velocities per ring, stated as PHYSICS rather than a copy of the code — the tangential component is preserved unconditionally, the approaching normal component is reflected exactly when the floor doesn't bind, the floor's own case is checked where it does, a receding ship is never pulled back, and every bounce without exception leaves outward speed ≥ the floor; with controls asserting both the reflection path and the floor path were genuinely exercised. **(D)** the floor's three measured cases. **(E)** twelve hazard fields byte-identical across a bounce, plus 300 real frames per ring proving the satellite never leaves its rail. **(F)** field debris and a large Hunter core still deflect, ship unmoved. **(G)** medium/small Hunters still die on the shield. **(H)** one cost, one deflect, one spied ping, energy clamped at 0. **(I)** a 12-node tow through 240 frames of bouncing, mid-world and across the seam. **(J)** wrap correctness — and the direction, not just the distance, because a naive normal still lands the ship exactly `radius + 2` away, just **on the wrong side**. **(K)** an `AudioSys.ctx === null` update-and-draw smoke.

**The suite is mutation-tested against SEVEN deliberate breakages and every one fails on BEHAVIOURAL assertions, not merely source pins** — two of them only after the suite was strengthened for exactly that reason. Floor removed → 5 behavioural (§D's three cases at 2 / 5 / 2, plus §C's floor-path controls). Restitution → 0 → 3. **Rail gate removed** (every hazard back to `shieldDeflect`) → **28**. Bounce also shoves the satellite → 7. **Naive non-wrap normal → 2**, catching that the ship was shoved at −115 px/s along the true normal, i.e. straight *into* the satellite. Reflecting the receding component too → 4. Doing the arithmetic in the world frame instead of the hazard's → 2.

**Prior session (CS021 P1, landed):** implemented CS021 Phase 1 (per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS021.md` P1, spec `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS021.md` §1/§4 and the resolved forks in §3), reading `CLAUDE.md`/`PLANNED-FEATURES-CS021.md`/`IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS021.md` in full and grepping every anchor in `asteroids-deluxe.html` by symbol rather than reading it whole. (`STATUS.md` is now 710 KB across 689 very long lines; the recent session log, Known issues, Balance notes, Next up and Playtest asks were read in full, the deep v2.0/v3.x history was not — noted rather than claimed otherwise.) **Corrections §2 were binding and were honoured: no ES module (the generator is a plain inlined function and the file still contains zero `export`/`import`/`require`), no new satellite-size constant (`DEBRIS_RADII[3] * 2` is read at the call site), no `DECISIONS.md`.**

**SIX EDITS, ALL IN `asteroids-deluxe.html`.** **(1) An `ORBIT_*` constants block** after the starfield constants: `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS` 180, `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` 150, `ORBIT_RING_COUNT` 4, `ORBIT_DENSITY` `[0.75, 0.45, 0.35, 0.85]`, `ORBIT_GAP_MULT` 2.5, `ORBIT_SAFETY_MARGIN` 8, `ORBIT_ANG_VEL` (6 °/s in radians), `ORBIT_FAST_MULT` 3.0, `ORBIT_FAST_RING` 3 (1-based), `ORBIT_SPAWN_TRIES` 60. **`ORBIT_LEVEL_EVERY = 3` deliberately does NOT live in that block** — it sits with `JUNK_CYCLE` inside the CS018 P1 level-table section, because `test-cs018-p1.js` §B slices the source from that section's banner to `levelDef`'s closing brace and evaluates it ALONE in a bare `vm` context; anything `levelDef` reads must be inside that slice or the purity proof throws. The in-file comment says exactly that, so the next person doesn't "tidy" it into the ORBIT block. **(2) `levelDef(n).archetype`** — `n % ORBIT_LEVEL_EVERY === 0 ? "orbit" : "field"`, a pure function of `game.wave`, no second clock. **(3) `generateOrbitLayout()` + four helpers** (`placeOrbitRing`, `nearestOrbitDist`, `spawnSafeOrbitLayout`, `orbitTangent`/`orbitSyncVelocity`) and **`spawnOrbitWave()`**, inlined above `nextWave()`. **(4) `nextWave()`'s archetype branch**, immediately after the existing `const count` / `const speedMul` pair: `"orbit"` calls `spawnOrbitWave(speedMul)`, `"field"` runs the shipped loop moved wholesale into the `else` (`git diff -w` reads as a pure indent). `junkCount` is not consumed on orbit levels; **no third `const speedMul = junkSpeedMul();` site was created** — the orbit branch reuses `nextWave`'s own local, so `test-cs018-p3.js` §C's "exactly TWO byte-identical sites" pin still holds unchanged. **(5) `DebrisSatellite.update()`'s optional orbit motion mode** — with `orbitCenter` absent the drift path is byte-for-byte what it was; with it present the ANGLE is integrated and x/y are DERIVED wrap-aware through `wrapPos`, and `vx`/`vy` are re-synced to the instantaneous tangent every frame so everything that only READS a debris velocity (the garbage kick in `destroyDebris`, `shieldDeflect`'s speed carry) sees the truth instead of a stale constructor drift. Sprite spin is untouched and independent. **(6) `destroyDebris()`'s tangent handoff** — `const tangent = orbitTangent(a);` above the split loop, children take `vx`/`vy` from it and carry NO orbit state (FORK-CS021-C2 → (i)); `tangent` is `null` for every field parent, so the scatter path is inert. **No `game`-object field was added, no `DEBUG_VARS` entry, no `localStorage` key touched, `GAME_VERSION` untouched at `"1.0.0.20"` (TRAP 1), and the GDD / `GDD-VERSION-HISTORY.md` / `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` are untouched (TRAP 2 — P2 owns the lever row, P5 owns the GDD).**

**THE SHIPPED GEOMETRY REPRODUCES SPEC §1.2 TO THE DIGIT, verified against the REAL generator rather than restated:** `minRequiredGap` **65** (the 2.5× multiplicative half beats the additive 26 + 8 = 34), `spacePerSatellite` **157**, and per ring — r=180: maxCount 7, count **6**, arc gap **96.50 px**; r=330: 13, **6**, **253.58**; r=480: 19, **7**, **338.85**; r=630: 25, **21**, **96.50**. **Total 40**, outermost satellite **edge 676 px** against the ~700 px wrap-clean budget (`WORLD_H/2 − 20`), inner-ring clearance over a wave-1 dock (radius 88) exactly **46 px**. Ring 3 is both the sparsest by lane width AND the fast one at 18 °/s (≈151 px/s tangential) against the others' 6 °/s (18.8 / 34.6 / 66.0 px/s at rings 1 / 2 / 4).

**THREE THINGS I HAD TO DECIDE, ALL SURFACED RATHER THAN QUIETLY PICKED — the first is the one that most wants Paul's eye.** **(a) SPAWN-SAFETY CLEARANCE IS MEASURED CENTRE-TO-CENTRE.** The spec says a banded ring "rerolls its `startAngle` … until the nearest satellite clears the ship by ≥ `minRequiredGap`" and provides a bounded-attempt escape. `minRequiredGap` is an EDGE-TO-EDGE lane width between two *adjacent satellites*, so a ship sitting exactly on a ring radius can never be more than half a lane from the nearest satellite's SURFACE — at ring 1 the best any rotation can achieve is 93.2 px centre-to-centre, i.e. **34.2 px surface-to-surface against a 65 px requirement**. Reading "clears by" as surface-to-surface therefore makes the reroll **unsatisfiable by construction** whenever the ship is near a ring, so the bound would exhaust on the *normal* path (I solved it: ring 4 fails for dock distances 542–620 px, ≈22% of all `Dock` placements) and the fallback would "accept the last roll" — which can put a satellite on top of the ship, the exact opposite of FORK-A(c)'s "safe by construction". Centre-to-centre IS satisfiable for all four rings at **every** distance `Dock` can produce (260–620 px), so the bound becomes genuine insurance: measured over 160 on-ring trials the worst case was **16 of 60 attempts**, and over 120 real orbit wave starts at random ship positions the closest satellite-to-ship distance was **66.32 px against the 65 px floor**, never below. That is the reading I shipped, it is documented at the function, and **if Paul meant something stricter it is a one-expression change in `spawnSafeOrbitLayout` — worth a look at the P4→P5 gate.** **(b) `archetype` READS THE UNCLAMPED `n`, not the level-63-clamped `L`** — the spec and the phase prompt both write `n % 3 === 0`, and they have to: reading `L` would make level 63 (63 % 3 === 0) and *every level after it* an orbit level forever, an orbit-only endgame. It is now the SECOND field on `levelDef` derived from `n` alongside `level`; `test-cs018-p1.js` §G's "field-identical to levelDef(63) except `level`" claim was repointed to name both exceptions and to assert positively that the every-3rd rhythm survives the plateau (64 field, 65 field, 66 orbit). `levelDef(Infinity)` → `NaN % 3 !== 0` → `"field"`, no throw. **(c) `logDifficultySnapshot(count, speedMul, …)` was left BYTE-UNCHANGED**, so on an orbit level the CSV's `junkCount` column reports the level TABLE's 3/5/9/13 value while 40 satellites actually spawned. Changing it would have broken `test-cs017-p2.js`'s row-shape pins and adding an `archetype` column is a `DIFFLOG_FIELDS` change, i.e. out of P1's scope — but **the dev CSV is now mildly misleading on 21 of 63 levels and P5's doc sweep (or a later phase) should decide whether that column follows the spawn or gains a sibling.** Every affected test was repointed to assert the column against `levelDef`, which is what it now means.

**⚠ SUPERSEDED BY CS021 P1b (see this session's entry above), which fixed exactly this the moment Paul read it — the paragraph is kept as written because it is the record of what P1 shipped and why. Two of its claims were also sharpened by P1b's measurements: the drain is 5 deflects in 0.10 s against a field rock's 1.38 s, and the ship only takes damage inside 59 px, not in the shield-only annulus.** — **AN EMERGENT INTERACTION, SHIPPED AS SPECCED AND FLAGGED FOR THE GATE: the rail is authoritative, so `shieldDeflect()` cannot push an orbiting satellite.** It sets `vx`/`vy` and shoves the body out of overlap; the next `update` re-derives the position from the orbit angle and puts it straight back. A shielded ship held against a ring therefore drains `SHIELD_HIT_COST` every frame and cannot bounce the satellite away — the rings read as heavy things on rails rather than as loose rocks. Nothing crashes, the fairness floor guarantees lanes wide enough to pass without touching, and **inventing "a deflect knocks a satellite off its rail" would have been new design**, so it was not invented. `test-cs021-p1.js` §E pins the behaviour explicitly (a satellite shoved 500 px is back on its ring one frame later) so a future change to it is deliberate. **Gate question 1 (fairness, especially inbound with a full tow) is where this wants judging.**

**FIRST-PASS VALUES THAT EXIST FOR THE GATE TO SETTLE (FORK-CS021-C1 is explicit that Paul tunes these live, and P3 turns both into debug knobs whose `def` derives from these constants):** `ORBIT_ANG_VEL` **6 °/s** — one full revolution per minute, ≈66 px/s tangential at ring 4, about a size-3 satellite's own drift speed — and `ORBIT_FAST_MULT` **3.0**, putting ring 3 at ≈151 px/s, between a size-2 (110) and a size-1 (160) debris. Neither is specced; both are chosen to be defensible starting points, not answers.

**FRAME-BUDGET PROBE — spec §8 item 7 / FLAG-CS021-h, REPORTED and deliberately NOT GATED** (`test-cs021-p1.js` §K, driving real `update(1/60)` frames with a progressive full harvest, one kill every 6 frames, the ship's hull topped up outside the timed region so a death can't silently drop the measurement into `updateDeath`'s much cheaper path — which it did on the first draft, and the §K validity assertions now pin `state === "playing"` and the level number so it can never regress to that):

| scenario | spawned | frames | PEAK ENTITIES (debris / garbage / particles) | update(dt) median | p95 | p99 | worst |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **level 3, ORBIT, full harvest** | 40 | 3,204 | **702** (360 / 256 / 160) | **0.275 ms** | 0.45 ms | 0.80 ms | 3.8 ms |
| level 4, field control (junkCount 13, the cycle's densest) | 13 | 1,134 | 420 (117 / 237 / 152) | 0.146 ms | 0.29 ms | 0.56 ms | 1.1 ms |
| **worst case: level 3 orbit, ship allowed to die** | 40 | — | **2,329** (360 / 1,233 / 1,314) | 0.237 ms | — | 0.72 ms | 1.3 ms |

**Orbit vs field: 1.67× peak entities, ~1.9× median frame cost, ~3× worst frame — and the absolute numbers are tiny.** A 0.275 ms median against a 16.7 ms 60 fps budget is ~1.6%; the worst single frame measured anywhere was 3.9 ms, and the load curve (median ms binned by simultaneous entity count) is essentially **flat** at 0.24 / 0.28 / 0.28 ms across the 100–250 / 250–500 / 500–1000 bands, i.e. `update()` cost is not scaling with the orbit level's population in any way that threatens a frame. The genuine peak — 2,329 simultaneous entities including **1,233 standing garbage** — happens when the ship dies on a part-harvested orbit level and `updateDeath`'s shockwave detonates the whole ring at once; that path is cheaper per frame, but 1,233 canisters is far past anything this build has carried before and it is the number the O(n²) `coalesceGarbage` pass (Known-issues FLAG D-2) would first show up at. **TWO CAVEATS, both real: this measures `update(dt)` ONLY — `draw()` is not in the loop, and the standing browser watch item is `shadowBlur` render cost, which scales with exactly these counts; and headless Node timing is GC-noisy, which is why medians and percentiles are reported and the bare mean is labelled as inflated.** Gate question 5 (any hitching during full harvest?) is the in-hand check against this.

**BASELINE MEASURED BEFORE EDITING, not inferred: 85 files, 31,702 assertions, 0 failures** — exactly the figure CS020 P2 recorded, so the tree started fully green. **Full regression after the phase, run twice consecutively: 86 files, 0 failures on both runs** (35,736 and 35,714 assertions). The only per-file difference between the two runs is `test-cs018-p4.js`'s **documented, pre-existing, RNG-dependent assertion COUNT** — see Known issues, where the measured spread is updated.

`startGame`/`update(1/60)`/`nextWave`/`destroyDebris`/`draw` path with `Math.random` stubbed by a seeded LCG; nothing under test is reimplemented. **(A)** `node --check` plus source pins: TRAP 1 satelliteDiameter` arithmetic, the total, the outer edge, the wave-1 dock clearance, and that ring 3 is simultaneously the widest-laned and the fastest. **(C)** the §8 items 1–4 sweep at **every** orbit level 3→63. **(D)** the field-level proof: 42 field levels each spawn exactly `junkCount` with **no orbit field of any kind** on any satellite, 21 orbit levels spawn 40 and provably do not consume `junkCount`, `levelDef` returns 14 fields with the `junkCount`/`payloadSlots`/`maxLargeHunters`/tier columns pinned against CS018 P1's own expectations, and the schedule holds at 3/6/9/60/63/66/99/2001. **(E)** the motion mode through 60 real frames: angle advance, position derived, still exactly on the ring wrap-aware, `|v| = angVel × radius`, sprite spin advancing independently at its own rate, ONE shared captured centre object equal to the dock's position, the rail-authority pin, and — the control that matters — a hand-built plain `DebrisSatellite` proving the drift path is `x += vx*dt` with `vx` untouched. **(F)** the split, from a parent on each of the four rings: child speed `=== orbitAngVel × orbitRadius`, child velocity **perpendicular to the centre ray** (dot product with the radial unit vector, < 1e-9), all four orbit fields `undefined` on the child, the child provably drifting OFF the ring on its next frame, and a field-level control where `orbitTangent` returns `null` and children keep their `junkSpeedMul`-derived drift. **(G)** the `maxCount ≥ 1` guard: three sub-minimum rings all REJECTED and recorded, total 0, plus the boundary radius where exactly one satellite fits with a **non-negative** gap, plus a mixed layout where only the unfair ring drops out. **(H)** wrap correctness at six edge/corner docks, 240 satellites, every toroidal distance exactly its ring radius (worst error 5.7e-13 px) **with a naive-arithmetic control showing the same measurement would be off by up to 2,665.7 px**, then the same end to end through a real seam-side `nextWave()`. **(I)** spawn safety: 160 trials with the ship seeded exactly on each ring radius (the reroll fired on 82 of them, worst 16 of 60 attempts, every ring cleared, no satellite ever inside `minRequiredGap`), a proof that a reroll moves start angles and NOTHING else (radius/count/maxCount/density/angVel/gap/angleStep all identical), a deliberately unsatisfiable layout proving the bound TERMINATES (every ring exhausts exactly 60 attempts, reports `cleared: false`, still returns a full usable satellite set, and logs exactly one warning per ring), and 120 real orbit wave starts at random ship positions. **(J)** determinism. **(K)** the frame-budget probe above. **(L)** an `AudioSys.ctx === null` smoke that both updates AND draws 240 frames of a real orbit level, then runs levels 4→7 back to back.

**SEVEN EXISTING TEST FILES NEEDED REPOINTING — the surface was wide, exactly as CS020 P1b's lesson predicted, and finding it took a full sweep rather than a grep.** All seven are repoints, not weakenings: in every case the original claim is preserved where it still applies and the orbit archetype gets its own assertion rather than a skip, and each carries a `REPOINTED BY CS021 P1` note explaining what split and why. **(1) `test-cs017-p1.js` §B** ("the ONE clock, proven by its effect") — the effect is now the archetype as well as the count, so it asserts both, plus that the archetype is a pure function of the level. **(2) `test-cs017-p1.js` §F** — the junk-count and tier-speed-envelope claim is a category error for a satellite on a rail, so orbit levels assert `|v| = angVel × radius` instead. **(3) `test-cs017-p2.js` §B** — the DiffLog `junkCount` column still matches `levelDef` at every level (unchanged); the "the level actually spawned junkCount pieces" sanity check split by archetype, and gained an orbit-state assertion in both directions. **(4) `test-cs017-p3.js` §B/§C/§D/§G** — the biggest one: the junk-cycle SHAPE claims (rise / reset / no spiral) now run over the level table's own column, which is what "the junk cycle" has always meant, while "the spawn really consumes it" is asserted at all 42 field levels and the 21 orbit levels assert their own count and orbit state; §C recovers the multiplier from a real spawned piece at field levels only; §D discovered the tangent handoff (its five orbit levels now assert speed inheritance and the absence of orbit state); §G's ceiling claim split the same way; and a new §B4b asserts the endgame plateau does NOT freeze the archetype. **(5) `test-cs018-p1.js` §G** — the 13-field count became 14 and the clamp exception list became `["level", "archetype"]`, with new positive assertions for 63 → orbit, 64/65 → field, 66 → orbit and the 1000 / 1e6 / Infinity extremes. **(6) `test-cs018-p3.js` §B** — split by archetype; also found that **all three phase-boundary levels (21, 42, 63) are themselves orbit levels**, since each is divisible by 3, so the "a phase's last level holds 13" claim is now checked in the table column it still lives in. **(7) `test-cs020-p1.js` §J** — the cross-build byte-identity control drove `toLevel(X, 12)` inside its seeded window, and **level 12 is now an orbit level**, so the fixed build and the pre-fix build at `09d443f` legitimately consume different amounts of `rand()` climbing to it and desynchronise before the delivery path is ever exercised. The climb moved OUT of the measured window onto its own fixed seed and the measurement seed is re-armed after `quiet()`, which keeps the control at full strength exactly where its claim lives. **(8) `test-v31-world.js` §B** — the "every debris spawn lands in the ship-relative ring" claim now runs on field levels only, with orbit levels asserted against their own invariant (toroidal distance to the DOCK equals the ring radius, outer edge inside the wrap-clean budget) and control assertions that both archetypes were genuinely sampled.

**FOUR THINGS SURPRISED ME.** (1) **The frame-budget probe was measuring almost nothing on its first draft.** A parked ship inside a 40-satellite ring level dies in seconds; once it does, `update()` drops into `updateDeath()` and then early-returns at `"gameover"`, so most of the "measured" frames were a nearly-empty code path. The tell was a load-curve bin reading 0.000 ms at 1,000–2,000 entities. Fixed by topping the hull up outside the timed region, and the §K validity assertions now pin `game.state === "playing"` and the level number so it can never silently regress. **Worth knowing for any future probe that parks a ship in a hazard.** (2) **All three phase-boundary levels are orbit levels.** 21, 42 and 63 are each divisible by 3, so the level table's one documented flat step (`rel === 21` holds 13 instead of restarting the cycle) is now invisible in the SPAWN at every one of the three places it occurs. Not a problem — the column is untouched — but it means the junk cycle's most distinctive feature and the orbit schedule are perfectly out of phase, which is a design fact nobody chose and worth knowing at the gate. (3) **`levelDef`'s bare-context purity proof genuinely constrained where a constant could live.** `ORBIT_LEVEL_EVERY` had to go inside the CS018 P1 section, not with the other ORBIT_* constants, because `test-cs018-p1.js` §B slices exactly that block and evaluates it with nothing else in scope. That is a good test doing its job, but it is an invisible constraint unless you have read that file. (4) **`test-starfield.js` §E has a pre-existing unseeded-RNG flake** — see Known issues; it is not caused by this phase and was measured on the unmodified `HEAD` build to prove it.
