Carry-forwards from P1/P2, still standing:

- ~~**P3's geometry only fits because of P1.**~~ **CONFIRMED IN P3, and it is now an assertion rather than a note:** the 1,334 px outer satellite edge clears the orbit world's 1,420 px budget and fails the field world's 700 px one, and `test-cs022-p3.js` §F carries that second half as a positive CONTROL — the geometry is proven not to fit the field world, so CS022 P1 can never be mistaken for cosmetic. The trap it protects against is real and caught two more victims in P3's sweep (`test-cs021-p1.js` §C and §H/§I).
- ~~**P3 will re-arm `orbitEffectiveCount`'s clamp.**~~ **HAPPENED, exactly as predicted and with no re-derivation needed:** at 460/276 a requested 5 walks back down to 4 (edge 1,610 px vs the 1,420 px budget), and `test-cs021-p3.js` §F is repointed onto that reality. `test-cs022-p2.js` §D's "documented arithmetic for a geometry that hasn't landed" is now cross-checked against the live constants.
- **`orbitRadiusStepFor(count)` is a trivial wrapper around `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP`** — radii are always `innerRadius + i * ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` at every ring count, and P3 leaned on exactly that.
- **P4's Architecture Map additions from P1**, so the doc sweep does not have to rediscover them: **Constants** row gains the `WORLD_SIZE_*` block (and the note that `WORLD_W`/`WORLD_H` are `let`, with the no-module-load-caching warning); **Flow functions** row gains `worldDims`, `worldSizeFor`, `applyWorldSize` and `resizeWorld`; **game object** row gains `game.worldSize`; the **draw()/starfield** material gains `starsActive` and `rebuildStarsActive`. The GDD's new **§2.11.1** is P4's, per its own prompt — nothing about the world size entered the GDD in P1 or P2 (TRAP 2 both times). **P4 should also retire the GDD §2.13.1 paragraph and the `orbitCount` registry row's description of the step-pad clamp** (both still describe P2's retired rule) once P3's real geometry lands and the doc sweep touches that section anyway.

**✅ CS021 IS COMPLETE — P1, P1b, P2, P3, P4 and P5 all landed; `GAME_VERSION` `"1.0.0.21"`.** **Next is CS022, and its opening scope is already decided by CS021's own playtest gate: the world grows and the orbit geometry moves out.** Three things belong in one CS022 planning conversation, because they are one decision and settling any of them alone will be wrong: **(1) world size** — gate Q1's spacing needs `min(WORLD_W, WORLD_H) ≥ 2708` against today's 2560×1440, which is the moment to design the per-level size scheme Paul named (size 1 = one screen, 4 = 2×2, 9 = 3×3, 16 = 4×4 — note 4 rings at Q1's spacing needs **size 16**, and size 9 fits only 3 rings by 2 px); **(2) ring geometry** — `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS` 460 / `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` 276 per Q1, with `orbitRadiusStepFor`'s fixed-outer-edge clamp and the `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP_PAD` floor both needing a re-look at the new scale; **(3) the density curve** — unasked-for but forced, since bigger circumferences at the shipped densities give **87** size-3s instead of 40, reopening FORK-CS021-D and FLAG-CS021-h together. Then **re-run the playtest gate** — Q2 through Q8 were never answerable and are still open, including Q8 (does the `COMBO n/N` readout read at a glance, and is the grace-window countdown wanted after all — spec §10 is explicit that the countdown is a FEATURE, not a retune). Everything below describes what CS021 shipped and stands as-is.

**CS021 P1 + P1b + P2 + P3 + P4 landed — the orbit-level archetype end to end, the shielded ship-bounce off rail-borne satellites, the occurrence-scaled fairness floor, the full ORBIT debug section + reroll, and now the HUD delivery-combo readout.** P1b is an in-round corrective phase opened by Paul in conversation (the CS020 P1b precedent) and is NOT in `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS021.md`; the numbered phases are otherwise unchanged. **P5's doc sweep must remember P1b exists** — the GDD needs `shieldBounce` in §2's shield/collision material and the Architecture Map's Constants row needs `SHIELD_BOUNCE_RESTITUTION`/`SHIELD_BOUNCE_MIN`, and neither is in P5's prompt because P1b post-dates it. Active plan docs: `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS021.md` / `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS021.md`, both at repo root. **Next is the ⛔ playtest gate** (P4 must be played through at least levels 3/6/9 and ideally one past the 1.8 floor at 24 before P5 runs — see Playtest asks below), **then P5** (retune, `GAME_VERSION` → `"1.0.0.21"`, doc sweep). **What CS021 P4 leaves for whoever touches the delivery combo or the HUD next:**

- **`HUD_COMBO_Y` (84) is a first-pass PLAYTEST KNOB, not a derived value** — unlike the HULL/CARGO rings' geometry, nothing else on screen constrains it; it was picked to sit under `LEVEL n` (62) without touching Score/Level's own bare-literal positions. If the gate wants it moved, it's a one-constant edit and the test suite reads the live constant rather than a hardcoded y, so retuning it cannot silently desync the suite.
- **The readout is intentionally NOT a second source of truth, and the test suite now proves that structurally, not just by inspection.** `test-cs021-p4.js` §A asserts NO assignment to any `game.*` field survives inside the new `drawHUD()` block — not just `deliveryCount`/`cargoMax` specifically. A narrower, field-specific version of that pin was tried first and missed a mutant that cached `game.deliveryCount` into a new field; if a future HUD element is ever tempted to cache a read for "efficiency," this is the pin shape that catches it and the one to reuse.
- **Nothing about the three termination routes (hook-reset / grace-expiry / `scatterChain`) was touched or needed touching.** The readout is `game.deliveryCount > 0`, full stop — if a FUTURE change ever adds a fourth way an effort can end (or changes what zeroes the counter), the readout tracks it for free as long as that change still writes to `game.deliveryCount`, and breaks for free (in the way that gets noticed) if it doesn't.
- **The grace-window countdown is still explicitly unbuilt (spec §10's own deferred detail).** If the gate asks for it, `game.comboGrace` already holds exactly the state a countdown would need (it's read once already, at the dock-offload block) — but per spec that's a FEATURE for a future changeset, not a P5 retune, and per CLAUDE.md's stop-and-surface rule any session that builds it should treat it as a genuine design addition, not silently fold it into a "P4.1."

**What CS021 P3 leaves for whoever touches the orbit archetype next:**

- **`applyDebug()` now runs every value through an optional per-entry `clampShown` hook before either map is written.** It is presently used by exactly one entry, `orbitCount` (FLAG-CS021-a) — a future knob with a similarly derived, non-literal range (e.g. anything else whose achievable max depends on OTHER live state) can reuse it rather than inventing a second mechanism. Every entry without one is byte-unchanged; `test-cs021-p3.js` §A/§B pin that.
- **`spawnOrbitWave()` now reads FIVE live `DEBUG.*` knobs directly** (`orbitSafetyMargin`/`orbitDensity1-5`/`orbitAngVel`/`orbitFastMult`/`orbitCount`) plus a caller-supplied `gapMult` (P2's seam, now fed by `orbitEffectiveGapMult()`). `radiusStep` is DERIVED from `DEBUG.orbitCount` via `orbitRadiusStepFor()`, never read as a knob itself — there is no `orbitRadiusStep` entry in the registry, by design (spec §6's table doesn't have one; the derivation IS the feature).
- **`orbitRadiusStepFor()` holds the OUTER EDGE fixed at 630 px (the shipped 4-ring edge) and redistributes evenly**, rather than deriving from the ~700 px wrap-clean budget. This is why it reproduces `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` bit-for-bit at the shipped `orbitCount` default — if a future changeset ever wants the outer edge itself to move with ring count, this function is where that assumption lives.
- **`game.orbitLayout` is now live game state, set unconditionally by BOTH `nextWave()` branches** (spawnOrbitWave's return / `null`), same treatment as `dock`. Anything that wants to know "is this an orbit level, and what does its layout actually look like right now" should read this rather than re-deriving from `game.debris`; `rerollOrbitStartAngles()` is the one existing consumer.
- **The reroll matches live entities to their ring by `orbitRadius` alone, in `game.debris`'s surviving creation order — no per-entity ring tag was added.** This works because ring radii are always distinct and entity removal never reorders survivors (the standing dead-flag + end-of-frame `.filter()` contract). If a future changeset ever gives two rings the SAME radius, or reorders `game.debris` mid-flight, this matching breaks silently; nothing currently does either.
- **A debug-panel raw keybind ("r") now exists outside the `bindings`/`REBINDABLE` system**, gated inline in the keydown listener same as the digit-entry capture. If a second such action is ever needed, it join the SAME block (debug-screen-only raw keys), not a new listener.

**What CS021 P2 leaves for whoever touches the orbit archetype next:**

- **`spawnOrbitWave()` now takes TWO arguments, `(speedMul, gapMult)`.** The generator itself (`generateOrbitLayout()`) is still pure and still has no idea occurrence exists — P2 pushed `orbitGapMult(game.wave)` in at the CALL SITE, exactly as P1's own note asked. P3's debug override slots in at the SAME call site (read the knob, fall back to `orbitGapMult(game.wave)` when the knob equals its `def`), not inside the generator.
- **`orbitGapMult(level)` lives beside `orbitTangent`/`orbitSyncVelocity`, right before `spawnOrbitWave()`** — not in the `ORBIT_*` constants block up top, and not in the CS018 level-table section either (unlike `ORBIT_LEVEL_EVERY`, it is never read by `levelDef()`, so it has no purity-slice constraint forcing it elsewhere).
- **The satellite total is no longer a flat 40.** It climbs 40 → 41 → 42 → 44 → 45 as occurrence rises from 1 to 8, then holds at 45 through level 63. Any future code (or test) that assumes "an orbit level always spawns 40" is wrong past level 9 — five existing test files made exactly that assumption and needed repointing this session (see the write-up above for the full list and the `orbitTotalAt()` helper pattern used in each).
- **The 1.8× clamp protects the REPORTED VALUE, not (at the shipped density curve) the GEOMETRY.** Measured, not assumed: `generateOrbitLayout`'s own additive floor (34 px) plus the shipped `ORBIT_DENSITY` curve topping out at 0.85 means no ring's `actualGapPx` ever drops below ~54 px even with the multiplier pushed arbitrarily low or negative. If a future density retune pushes any ring toward 1.0, re-check this — the geometric floor could start binding for real. `test-cs021-p2.js` §E's mutant check is shaped around the value-level violation, which does have teeth.
- **Real spawn safety at deep occurrences was checked, not assumed, and holds.** A genuinely-random 200-trial probe at levels 24/30/63/99/300 found worst ship-to-satellite clearance 47.15–49.45 px against the 46.8 px floor — consistent with P1's own level-3 measurement. A `console.warn("ring 4 exhausted...")` seen in `test-cs017-p3.js`'s constant-RNG (`PIN = 0.5`) harness is a property of that harness (a pinned reroll never explores a different angle), not a real-gameplay finding.

**What CS021 P1 leaves for whoever touches the orbit archetype next:**

- **The generator is PURE apart from `rand()` — every number comes from the call site.** `spawnOrbitWave()` is the only place the shipped `ORBIT_*` constants meet `generateOrbitLayout()`, which is exactly what P2 (swap the fixed `ORBIT_GAP_MULT` for the occurrence-scaled value) and P3 (swap each constant for its `DEBUG.*` knob) need. **Do not push a constant read down into the generator**; that is the seam both later phases turn on.
- **`ORBIT_LEVEL_EVERY` lives in the CS018 P1 level-table block, NOT in the ORBIT_* block, and that is load-bearing.** `test-cs018-p1.js` §B slices the source from that section's banner to `levelDef`'s closing brace and evaluates it alone in a bare `vm` context; a `levelDef` that reads anything outside the slice throws there. Moving the constant "where it belongs" breaks the purity proof.
- **`archetype` reads the UNCLAMPED `n`.** It is the second field on `levelDef` to do so, alongside `level`. Anything that assumes "levelDef(64+) is field-identical to levelDef(63) except `level`" is now wrong by one field — `test-cs018-p1.js` §G is the assertion that names both.
- **Spawn-safety clearance is CENTRE-TO-CENTRE, and the reasoning is in the function's comment.** A surface-to-surface reading is unsatisfiable by construction for a ship on a ring radius (ring 1 tops out at 34.2 px surface clearance against a 65 px floor) and would make the bounded loop exhaust on the normal path. If the floor ever needs to bite harder, change the METRIC deliberately and re-run `test-cs021-p1.js` §I, which measures the attempt counts.
- **The rail is authoritative — nothing can push an orbiting satellite, and the collision path is built around that rather than fighting it.** Pinned by `test-cs021-p1.js` §E. CS021 P1b is what that implies for the shield: `shieldBounce()` moves the SHIP, `shieldDeflect()` is left byte-untouched for every hazard that CAN be pushed, and the two are separated at one call site by `h.orbitCenter` — **the same field the motion mode gates on, deliberately, so there is one concept and not two.** Any future hazard that becomes rail-borne gets the right behaviour for free by carrying that field; anything that needs a *different* rule needs a different gate, not a widened one. If a changeset ever wants a satellite knocked OFF its rail, that is new design, not a fix.
- **A reroll must move START ANGLES ONLY.** `placeOrbitRing()` exists precisely so P3's reroll keybind can rebuild one ring without re-randomising the layout; `test-cs021-p1.js` §I already asserts radius/count/maxCount/density/angVel/gap/angleStep are untouched by one.
- **`ORBIT_ANG_VEL` (6 °/s) and `ORBIT_FAST_MULT` (3.0) are first-pass values with no spec behind them.** FORK-CS021-C1 says explicitly that Paul settles both live. P3's knob `def`s must DERIVE from these constants, never duplicate the numbers.
- **The dev difficulty CSV's `junkCount` column reports the level TABLE, not the spawn, on orbit levels.** `logDifficultySnapshot` was left byte-unchanged (changing it is a `DIFFLOG_FIELDS` change and would break `test-cs017-p2.js`'s row-shape pins). It is mildly misleading on 21 of 63 levels; P5's doc sweep or a later phase should decide whether that column follows the spawn or gains an `archetype` sibling.
- **All three phase boundaries (levels 21, 42, 63) are orbit levels**, since each is divisible by 3. The level table's one documented flat step (`rel === 21` holds 13 rather than restarting the junk cycle) is therefore invisible in the spawn at every place it occurs. Nobody chose that; it falls out of 21 % 3 === 0. Worth knowing before anyone reasons about the junk cycle's felt shape.

**CS020 IS COMPLETE — all phases landed, per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS020.md`.** P2 (retune + version bump + doc sweep) landed the session it shipped in — see the phase log above for the full breakdown; the playtest gate between P1b and P2 was cleared with all four questions answered "no change" (Q3, the cap, came back "Better" — positive feedback with no code implication), so no delivery-combo constant moved. **CS021 is now the open changeset (see above).** **The CS020 archive move IS already done** — verified by `ls archive/` in CS021 P1: `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS020.md` and `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS020.md` are both there and repo root now holds only the CS021 pair, so `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS021.md`'s P5 item 5 ("verify, do not re-run") will find nothing to do.

- **What CS020 P1b leaves for whoever touches the delivery combo next.** (a) **The counter is bounded STRUCTURALLY, not by a clamp** — `deliveryCount <= game.cargoMax` falls out of the towed-hook reset plus the pickup gate's `cargoMax` test, and there is deliberately no `Math.min` anywhere. Any future code that increments the counter outside the offload block, or that lets a chain exceed `cargoMax`, silently breaks the guarantee; `test-cs020-p1b.js` §F is the assertion that will catch it. (b) **The two rules are load-bearing TOGETHER and neither is sufficient.** The window is generous *because* the hook reset bounds the counter — widening the window is safe, dropping or ungating the reset is not. `test-cs020-p1b.js` §H is the annulus-farm assertion that fails the moment the reset stops discriminating. (c) **The `!inRing` guard on the reset is the fairness half.** Resetting on every hook makes a magnet grab at the dock kill the player's own run mid-offload — pinned by §G's ±1 px pair. (d) **The re-arm is at the RING (`+40`), not the `+10` offload radius**, on purpose: once inside the neighbourhood the run is safe indefinitely so the player can take their time closing the last 30 px. §D's 12-second annulus loiter is what fails if that moves. (e) **`DOCK_COMBO_GRACE` and `DEBUG_VARS.dockComboGrace.def` must move TOGETHER** (`def` is the const × 1000); §A asserts they agree, which is exactly the mismatch P2's retune could otherwise introduce.

- **What CS020 P1 leaves for whoever touches the dock next.** (a) **Chain nodes now carry a `towed` field, and its default is `!== false`, not truthiness.** Any new code that constructs a chain node must either set it or accept the TOWED default deliberately; any new code that READS it must keep the `!== false` form, or the 22-file hand-seeded-node surface silently reclassifies. (b) **The tag radius and the combo-reset radius are one constant on purpose** (`DOCK_NEIGHBORHOOD_PAD`). Splitting them apart re-opens the farmable annulus; if either ever needs to move, both move. (c) **The `+10` offload radius is a genuinely different number** and was left as a bare literal deliberately — do not "finish the job" by hoisting it into the same constant. (d) **Everything keyed on `game.deliveryCount` is now correct by construction**, because incidentals never advance it. Do not add a second guard at the SMD trigger, the P8 reward tiers or either achievement latch; that would create two sources of truth for one rule.

- **`GDD-VERSION-HISTORY.md` has no CS018 entry.** Noticed while appending this session's CS019 entry (grepped, not found) — CS018 P10's own doc sweep never added a consolidated round entry for CS018, unlike every round before and after it. Not backfilled this session (out of a bug-fix changeset's scope); worth a one-line administrative fix whenever a future session is touching that file for its own reasons.

## Playtest asks (Paul — can't be checked headlessly)

**CS024 P6b ADDS NO NEW GATE, BUT IT ADDS ONE ASK TO THE GATE B SESSION: GET PAST LEVEL 33.** The defect P6b fixed — a small UFO that was *slower* at level 33 than at level 25 — is invisible below L25, and the stagger it replaced it with (speed → fire rate → evasiveness → shot velocity → accuracy, saturating L33 → L65) is invisible below L33. Gate B's seven questions are unchanged and still answerable in its stated 1 → 20 window; this is an addition, not a replacement. **If you do reach the 25–45 band, the single most useful thing to report is whether the UFOs read as continuously escalating, with no point where they feel like they got easier.** `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §4 has the full five-question version for CS025's own gate later.

**⛔ CS024's GATE B IS OPEN AND BLOCKING. CS024 P7 MUST NOT RUN until the seven questions below are answered here.** All code phases (P1–P6d) are landed. The questions are reproduced **verbatim** from `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS024.md`'s "⛔ GATE B" section, which is the single source; write the answers inline under each one (`Paul says: …` is the established style), or paste them into the next session's opening message and tell it to record them here first. **⛔ WHAT TO PLAY: LEVELS 1 → 45.** That is up from the "1 → 20, ideally 30" this block used to say, and the extra range is not padding: **CS024 P6b's UFO stagger does its work between L33 and L65** (speed saturates L33, rate of fire L41, evasiveness L49, shot velocity L57, accuracy L65), and the junk and hunter chains do not finish either (junk speeds saturate **L41**, hunter speeds **L33**). A run that stops at 30 sees roughly the first half of one chain and none of the stagger.

**CS024 P6d's `startLevel` debug knob (GLOBAL section, registry) can shortcut the spot-check.** Rather than playing 40+ minutes to reach L41–L99, drag `startLevel` to the level in question (e.g. 41, 65, 90) and start a fresh run — `leverState`/`liveLevers` land exactly where a played-through run would, since every lever is a pure function of `game.wave`. **Any run started this way voids recording** (a `"DEBUG RUN"` HUD marker confirms it; no high score, no achievement, no lifetime-stat write) and gives a level-N field with a level-1 SHIP — no scoop upgrades, no banked powerups, no towed load. That's fine for judging saturation/pacing/tuning questions (7, 8, 9, and the flatness-past-L41 question below), which are about the field, not the player's build — but for anything that reads on the ship's accumulated state (scoop level, powerup bank size, chain length), a real played-through run is still the only honest source, and the two should not be conflated when recording an answer below.

**THE STANDING INSTRUCTION, RESTATED BECAUSE IT IS WHAT MAKES THE ANSWERS ACTIONABLE: for anything driven by a debug slider, open the panel, retune live, and REPORT THE NUMBER YOU LANDED ON — NOT A YES/NO.** "Too fast" costs the next session a guess; "I settled at 140" costs it nothing. Gate A proved this works: Q1 came back with three numbers and all three shipped verbatim into P4. **"Fine" is also a complete answer** — a gate that comes back clean means P7 is bump-and-sweep only, which has happened twice before (CS020 P2, CS022 P4). Do not manufacture changes to justify the gate.

**Gate B's seven questions, verbatim:**

**7. Does the sawtooth read as *breathing*, or as the game repeatedly getting easier?** This is the changeset's central bet.

Paul says:

**8. Is the carry legible?** When `junkCount` resets to 3 and the satellites are visibly faster, does that land as an escalation?

Paul says:

**9. Three chains breathing on different periods** — `junkCount` every 10 levels, `coalescePause` every 8, `ufoAppearFreq` every 8. Rich, or arrhythmic?

Paul says:

**10. Any lever whose floor or ceiling is wrong.** Slide it, land on a number, report the number.

Paul says:

**11. Engine-as-fuel:** does 5 seconds of thrust feel like a powerup or a tease?

Paul says:

**12. Count-only powerups:** does losing timed mode make Magnet (40 hooks) or Rapid (40 shots) feel meaningfully different?

Paul says:

**13. Level 12 and the Super Mega Delivery** — now that 24 slots is the first moment an SMD is possible at all, does hitting it land as the payoff it should be?

Paul says:

**⛔ THE BRIEFING — WHAT CHANGED UNDER THIS GATE IN CS024 P6c, BECAUSE THE PANEL YOU WILL OPEN IS NOT THE ONE THIS GATE WAS WRITTEN FOR.** The previous version of this block described **one flat slider per lever**, showing `auto`, which pinned that lever to a constant the moment you touched it. **That is gone.** A lever is a floor, a ceiling and a step count, so it now has **three rows each — 51 in all** — and moving any one of them re-derives that lever's whole ramp immediately, at every level.

**The three rows, and what each one actually does:**

- **`· floor`** — the value the lever holds at **level 1**, and the value it returns to **exactly** on every wrap. Move this to change where the ramp *starts*.
- **`· ceil`** — the value at the lever's **top step**. For a **dependent** that is where it plateaus and stays forever; for a **driver** it is the last rung before it sawtooths back to floor. Move this to change where the ramp *ends*.
- **`· steps`** — how many discrete positions the ramp has, **minimum 2**, integers only. For a **driver** this is its **period**: `junkCount` at 10 steps wraps every 10 levels, at 7 it wraps every 7. For a **dependent** it is how many carries it needs to saturate. **This is the knob for "the ramp is the wrong LENGTH"** — the thing that previously could only be reported in words. Changing it moves your position in the odometer discontinuously, so **start a fresh run after changing a step count.**

**⛔ FLOOR EQUAL TO CEIL PINS A LEVER FLAT AT EVERY LEVEL.** That is exactly what the retired single row did, so nothing is lost — it is now one thing you can choose rather than the only thing a slider can do. Useful for isolating one quantity's feel; **not** what you want for answering Q10.

**⛔ SEVEN LEVERS ARE INVERTED, THEIR ROWS ARE LABELLED `(inv)`, AND A FLOOR NUMERICALLY ABOVE ITS CEIL IS CORRECT.** `coalescePause` (5 → 1.5 s), `ufoAppearFreq` (25 → 12 s), both `ufoFireFreq*`, both `ufoDirChange*` and `ufoAccuracySmall` all **descend**, because a shorter delay and a smaller aim error are HARDER. **An inverted lever's floor is its EASY end.** There is no floor ≤ ceiling validator anywhere in this build and there must never be one — and as of P6c the slider ranges are deliberately wide enough to drag either endpoint **past** the other, which is a supported configuration, not a mistake. (P5's sliders could not do this at all: `coalescePause`'s 5.0 s floor was also its slider's maximum.)

**THE PANEL SHOWS THE CHAIN.** In each of the JUNK / HUNTER / UFO sections, a **`▼`** row unindented is that chain's **DRIVER** — everything indented below it moves when the driver wraps. A **`  ↳`** row is a **DEPENDENT**: it never advances on the level clock at all, only on its driver's wraps. A row with **neither glyph nor indent** is a flat knob belonging to no chain (`Small UFO chance`, `Hunter last-stand speed`, the GARBAGE / CHAIN GUARD / DELIVERY / POWERUPS / GLOBAL rows) — **those are not part of Q10.** The glyphs are derived from the table, so they cannot disagree with the mechanism.

**THE FULL TABLE, computed from the shipped build rather than from the spec.** "Sat." is the level at which a dependent first reaches its ceiling and plateaus; a driver has none — it wraps forever, every `steps` levels.

| # | Lever id — panel label | Floor → ceil | Steps | Role | Slider range (floor & ceil rows) | ◄► step | L1 | L20 | L45 | Sat. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `junkCount` — `▼ Junk count` | 3 → 12 | 10 | **DRIVER**, wraps every 10 levels; carries to all three junk speeds | [0, 21] | 1 | 3 | 12 | 7 | wraps |
| 2 | `junkSpeedLarge` — `↳ Junk speed lg` | 60 → 110 px/s | 5 | carried | [10, 160] | 12.5 | 60 | 72.5 | 110 | L41 |
| 3 | `junkSpeedMedium` — `↳ Junk speed md` | 95 → 165 px/s | 5 | carried | [25, 235] | 17.5 | 95 | 112.5 | 165 | L41 |
| 4 | `junkSpeedSmall` — `↳ Junk speed sm` | 140 → 240 px/s | 5 | carried | [40, 340] | 25 | 140 | 165 | 240 | L41 |
| 5 | `coalescePause` — `▼ Coalesce delay (inv)` | 5 → 1.5 s **(INV)** | 8 | **DRIVER**, wraps every 8 levels; carries to both hunter speeds | [0, 8.5] | 0.5 | 5 | 3.5 | 3 | wraps |
| 6 | `hunterSpeedMedium` — `↳ Hunter speed md` | 60 → 110 px/s | 5 | carried | [10, 160] | 12.5 | 60 | 85 | 110 | L33 |
| 7 | `hunterSpeedSmall` — `↳ Hunter speed sm` | 90 → 160 px/s | 5 | carried | [20, 230] | 17.5 | 90 | 125 | 160 | L33 |
| 8 | `ufoAppearFreq` — `▼ Appear interval (inv)` | 25 → 12 s **(INV)** | 8 | **DRIVER**, wraps every 8 levels; carries to **all nine** other UFO levers | [0, 38] | 1.86 | 25 | 19.43 | 17.57 | wraps |
| 9 | `ufoFlightSpeedBig` — `↳ Flight speed lg` | 100 → 150 px/s | 5 | carried | [50, 200] | 12.5 | 100 | 125 | 150 | L33 |
| 10 | `ufoFlightSpeedSmall` — `↳ Flight speed sm` | 150 → 210 px/s | 5 | carried | [90, 270] | 15 | 150 | 180 | 210 | L33 |
| 11 | `ufoFireFreqBig` — `↳ Fire mult lg (inv)` | 1.8 → 0.7 × **(INV)** | 6 | carried | [0, 2.9] | 0.22 | 1.8 | 1.36 | 0.7 | L41 |
| 12 | `ufoFireFreqSmall` — `↳ Fire mult sm (inv)` | 1.8 → 0.6 × **(INV)** | 6 | carried | [0, 3] | 0.24 | 1.8 | 1.32 | 0.6 | L41 |
| 13 | `ufoDirChangeBig` — `↳ Zig gap lg (inv)` | 2.2 → 1 s **(INV)** | 7 | carried | [0, 3.4] | 0.2 | 2.2 | 1.8 | 1.2 | L49 |
| 14 | `ufoDirChangeSmall` — `↳ Zig gap sm (inv)` | 1.8 → 0.7 s **(INV)** | 7 | carried | [0, 2.9] | 0.18 | 1.8 | 1.43 | 0.88 | L49 |
| 15 | `ufoShotSpeedBig` — `↳ Shot speed lg` | 300 → 430 px/s | 8 | carried | [170, 560] | 18.57 | 300 | 337.14 | 392.86 | L57 |
| 16 | `ufoShotSpeedSmall` — `↳ Shot speed sm` | 320 → 470 px/s | 8 | carried | [170, 620] | 21.43 | 320 | 362.86 | 427.14 | L57 |
| 17 | `ufoAccuracySmall` — `↳ Aim error sm (inv)` | 30 → 8 ° **(INV)** | 9 | carried | [0, 52] | 2.75 | 30 | 24.5 | 16.25 | L65 |

**Four things about that table worth knowing BEFORE you play, because each one will otherwise look like a bug.** **(1) THE SATURATION COLUMN IS WHY THE PLAY RANGE MOVED TO 45.** CS024 P6b restaged the nine UFO dependents so they saturate in a deliberate ORDER — speed (L33), then rate of fire (L41), then evasiveness (L49), then shot velocity (L57), and **accuracy last (L65)** — the UFOs get *faster* before they get *accurate*. At L20 six of those nine have not moved past their second carry; at L45 you have seen most of it. **The unevenness is the design and must not be "tidied" into one number.** **(2) `ufoAppearFreq` CYCLES FOREVER and never permanently tightens** — level 100 reads exactly as level 1. That is deliberate: it is the chain's driver, and a driver that stopped cycling would freeze all nine levers under it. UFO *pressure* escalates through those nine; UFO *rhythm* stays constant. **(3) The three chains breathe on 10 / 8 / 8, which is Q9's whole subject:** `junkCount` resets at L11, L21, L31, L41…; `coalescePause` and `ufoAppearFreq` both at L9, L17, L25, L33, L41… — the two 8-period chains reset together and the 10-period one drifts against them, meeting at L41. **Q9 is now answerable with a NUMBER**: if 8 and 8 landing together reads as a lurch, drag one of them to 7 or 9 on its `· steps` row and say which you preferred. **(4) `Junk count` is a COUNT, and the panel will happily show you a fraction on its floor/ceil rows** (they are ordinary sliders); the spawn rounds to the nearest whole satellite, and the difficulty log records what actually spawned.

**WHAT Q10 IS NOW ASKING.** Not "is a floor or a ceiling wrong" but any of three things, each with its own slider: **the ramp starts in the wrong place** (`· floor`), **ends in the wrong place** (`· ceil`), or **takes the wrong number of levels to get there** (`· steps`). Report the id and the number. `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS024.md`'s copy of Q10 still says step counts are not sliders — that wording is stale as of P6c and P7's doc sweep owns it (Known issues).

**WHAT Q11 AND Q12 ARE ACTUALLY ASKING, given what P6 changed under them.** **Q11 (Engine-as-fuel):** the tank is **5 seconds of FORWARD THRUST**, not 5 seconds of wall time — rotating, coasting, firing and drifting all cost nothing, so in practice one pickup lasts far longer than five seconds of play, and how much longer depends entirely on how much you hold thrust. The effect while it lasts is unchanged (towed mass halved, so thrust, top speed and the momentum tug all lighten) and it is **flat, not tapering** — the last tenth of a second feels exactly like the first. Two live sliders under the **POWERUPS** header: `engineBurnSeconds` (default **5.0**, range 0.5–60, step 0.5) and `engineMassMult` (default **0.5**, range 0–1, step 0.05 — LOWER is stronger). **Q12 (count-only):** Rapid and Triple were already count-capable but shipped timed, so what changed for most runs is that they now end when you spend them rather than when a clock runs out — 40 pulls and 30 pulls, and a Triple 3-fan is ONE pull. Magnet is 40 hooked canisters, counted at the hook (a clump costs one per piece). The chain guard is 3 intercepts. **All of them BANK now, including Engine and Guard for the first time** — a second pickup adds a full lot on top of what's left rather than refreshing, and the HUD ring shows the surplus as a thin halo outside the main ring.

**✅ CS024's GATE A IS CLOSED — answered by Paul, and P4 has run on those answers.** **Outcome: five of six came back "fine" and carried no code; Q1 came back with a RETUNE, and it is baked into the build as of CS024 P4** — `GARBAGE_COALESCE_DELAY` 3.0 → **5.0 s**, `GARBAGE_MAGNET_RANGE` 180 → **160 px**, `GARBAGE_MAGNET_PULL` 40 → **30 px/s²**, all three exactly as reported, all three on their own slider's grid. **Q1 came back the OPPOSITE way round from the failure the question was written to catch:** it asked "do hunters appear at all?" and the answer was "yes, and too fast" — permanent garbage (P3) removed the pressure that the 3.0 s inert window had been tuned against, and nobody predicted that. **One thing worth carrying forward:** the new 5.0 s delay is *exactly* the `coalescePause` lever's `floor`, so P5's wiring keeps level 1 at the value the gate validated and the lever's job is to shorten it from there. `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §2.5's parenthetical "180 px / 40 px/s²" is now stale — P7's doc sweep owns it (see Known issues). Kept below for the record; nothing here is outstanding.

**⛔ CS024's GATE A (the original questions, for the record) — P3 was landed and P4 was blocked until these six were answered here.** The questions live in `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §6. Below each is the headless answer already in hand, so the gate can concentrate on what only playing can settle. **Q1 (do Hunters still appear at all? play at least 6 levels without deliberately farming — roughly how many levels before the first one?) is the one that can change the plan.** Headlessly, the pipeline works end to end and works at every level: 12 co-located canisters become a large core in well under a second of simulated time, at level 1 as readily as level 63. What headless cannot tell you is whether canisters in an actual played field ever get close enough, for long enough, to reach 12 — the inert window is 3 s per piece, the mutual attraction range is 180 px in a 2560×1440 world, and you will be shooting and scooping them the whole time. **If the answer is "never," say so plainly and P4 inherits a coalescence retune it does not currently carry.** Note also that levels 2–4 can now produce a Hunter where the retired cap table forbade it (see Known issues) — an early Hunter is the new system working, not a regression. 
Paul says: Yes, hunters are formed. However, they are formed too quickly. I used debug knobs to come up with a better feel. I changed Garbage attraction delay to 5,000 ms, Garbage radius to 160px, and Garbage force to 30 px per second (squared) Please confirm if you are able to make these the default values.

**Q2 (is the field readable? does a level-6 screen read as salvage-rich or as visual noise?) has arithmetic behind it worth knowing before you look.** One fully-harvested large satellite line yields 39 canisters; a full Hunter lineage yields 18; nothing removes any of them but you and the dock. At a level-1 `junkCount` of 3 that is ~117 canisters from the satellites of a single level — over half the 220 ceiling. 
Paul says: Q2 is fine. 

**Q3 (does the cull ever visibly fire? you should never see a canister vanish) is the direct A/B on that:** the cull is silent by design — no blink, no particle, no sound, verified — so if you catch one disappearing, `GARBAGE_SOFT_MAX` is too low. Both are live sliders in the debug panel (`Garbage soft max`, `Garbage hard max`, GARBAGE section) — **retune in-session and report the number you landed on, not a yes/no.** 
Paul says Q3 is fine, overriding your request for a number. 

**Q4 (frame rate, subjectively, at the worst moment you can produce) has a headless half already answered, and it is the half that bounds the retune.** The O(n²) coalescence walk costs 24,090 pair visits per frame at the 220 ceiling and 44,850 at the 300 backstop — both measured exactly, with a deterministic counter rather than a clock. **The cost is quadratic in the ceiling: 450 pieces would be ~99,900/frame and 1,000 would be ~500,000.** So if the answer to Q3 is "raise it," raising to ~300–350 is affordable and beyond that is not — at which point the spatial grid recorded for CS025 becomes the real answer rather than a bigger number. 
Paul says: Q4 is fine. 

**Q5 (does removing the bonus canister leave the early levels flat?) is a straight subtractive question with no headless component.** It was explicitly a reward-flavoured reason to keep playing the low-stakes early waves, worth 250 points at the scoop and inviting greed by fattening the chain. Nothing replaces it in this changeset.
Paul says: Q5 is fine. 

**Q6 (last stand: does a large Hunter resuming its old vector when debris reappears read as intentional, or as a bug?)** — this is unchanged behaviour that the phase deliberately preserved rather than tidied: the moment debris reappears the steering block simply stops executing and the core keeps whatever velocity it held at that instant, with no disarm and no restore. It is now a documented exception rather than an accident, and its speed is a live slider (`Hunter last-stand speed`, HUNTER section, default 50 px/s). 
Paul says: Q6 is fine. 

**⛔ CS023's PLAYTEST GATE IS NOW OPEN AND BLOCKING — all code phases (P1–P4b) are landed, and P5 must not run until these are answered here.** The fifteen questions live in `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS023.md` under "⛔ PLAYTEST GATE"; write the answers into this section, and **report the number you landed on, not a yes/no**, for anything driven by a slider. Play levels **3, 6, 9 and 12** for one full pass of the ramp, plus **21** for a full field component on top, plus at least one FIELD level now that P4b makes the drift live there too (Q14 below). **Eight things are live sliders in the hidden Debug panel's ORBIT section: the four densities, `orbitAngVel`, `orbitFastMult`, and `debrisBounceRestitution` and `debrisDriftAccel`** (the last renamed from `orbitGravityAccel` by P4b, spec C15 — same slider, same position, new id; a value saved under the old id was reset to default once).

**⛔ THE ONE PIECE OF SETUP THE GATE ACTUALLY REQUIRES: play at least one level to a FULL HARVEST, orbit OR field.** The inward drift's trigger fires only once **nothing at all** is left inside 1,000 px of the dock (P4C: was 814 px — the trigger followed the rings out) — the whole population, plus every split child still in that radius, on EITHER archetype now (P4b, C15 — the gate that used to make this orbit-only is gone). A level played to a normal finish never shows the drift, so questions **11 (does it read at all)**, **12 (is the pull fast enough, is the cap right)** and **13 (do the interrupts read as knocking something off course)** are simply unanswerable without one full harvest. `debrisDriftAccel` at **0** disables the drift outright and is the A/B.

What CS023 P4/P4b have left you looking at, with the headless answers already in hand so the gate can concentrate on what only playing can settle:

- **The drift is slow on purpose, and the number to move first is the acceleration, not the cap (Q11/Q12).** REVISED BY P4C: at the shipped 30 px/s² a piece needs **10.5 s and 1,645 px of fall** to reach the now-314.2 px/s ceiling, so **the cap only binds for pieces starting beyond ~2,445 px from the dock — and NEITHER WORLD IS THAT BIG** (orbit's farthest point is 2,203 px, the field's 1,469). The longest falls either archetype allows are 1,403 px (orbit, arriving ~290 px/s) and 669 px (field, ~200 px/s), so **the cap never binds anywhere in the shipped game**, which is pure headroom for this question: raise `debrisDriftAccel` to ~35 px/s² before it starts limiting even the longest fall. If the field feels like it is taking forever to come back, that is the **acceleration**, and `debrisDriftAccel` is the slider — on either archetype. Headless says the arithmetic is exact to 1e-9 and that a piece released at 800 px coasts on more than 200 px further rather than parking; whether that reads as *the level re-forming around you* is the question, and now it is a question for a field level too, not only an orbit one.
- **The cap is derived, so question 4's answer moves it (Q4/Q12).** **314.2 px/s** (P4C: was 255.7 — it followed ring 4's radius out to 1,000 px) is ring 4's own tangential speed at the shipped `ORBIT_ANG_VEL` 6 °/s and `ORBIT_FAST_MULT` 3.0 — the fastest anything moves in orbit, which is the whole justification for the number, and it stays the SAME number on a field level (it derives from constants, never from the live layout). **If you retune either of those two velocity knobs at the gate, say so explicitly, because the drift's ceiling moves with them, on every level** and P5 has to record that. There is deliberately no separate slider for the cap, and no field-level variant of it either.
- **The interrupts are all real mechanics you already have (Q13).** Shooting a drifting piece, ramming one, watching two collide, or watching a UFO take one out — all four release it, and it keeps whatever velocity it had accumulated. Headless proves each one fires identically on a field level as on an orbit one; what it cannot tell you is whether the release *reads* as knocking something off course or just as the pull quietly switching off. A piece that has been drifting a while is moving fast enough to be legible, which is the bet.
- **`debrisBounceRestitution` is a live slider (Q9).** Default 1.0 (a perfectly elastic bounce, mirroring the shield's). Lower it if the shell reads like a pinball table; range 0–1.5, step 0.05. `DEBRIS_MASS`'s 9:3:1 is **not** a knob and should not become one — it is fixed by mass conservation through the split the game already performs, and it is what makes a small ricochet off a large.
- **Q14 IS ANSWERED BY THE CODE NOW, NOT LEFT FOR THE GATE: field levels get the drift too.** P4's own gate framed this as an open question ("is a field level too small to feel the pull, or does it want the mechanic as well?"); Correction C15 retracted the orbit-only recommendation that question rested on — measured against the real worlds, a field level's dead zone beyond the trigger (46%) is comparable to an orbit level's (75%), so the premise that it would be "on almost permanently" was wrong. **What the gate should now answer instead is whether the field-level FEEL matches the orbit-level one** (same trigger, same target, same acceleration, cap never binding) — not whether it should exist there.

- **The fast ring arrives at level 6, not level 3 (Q6)** — `ORBIT_FAST_RING` is ring 3, which the ramp doesn't lay until occurrence 2. Emergent, not designed, and Paul has already confirmed he wants it kept; the gate is only asking how it lands. Paul says: this is fine.
- **Q7 (frame rate in a browser) is the one the headless gate genuinely cannot answer, and P3's measurements narrow it usefully.** `update(dt)` at the 84-satellite peak is a 0.674 ms median with 1,158 peak entities — a non-issue. What is untested is `draw()`: `shadowBlur` scales with exactly these counts, and the moment to watch is a full harvest at level 21+ and the instant of death on a part-harvested orbit level (9,374 entities), which is where the standing-garbage peak lands. Paul says: this is fine
- **Q3 (the world change itself) and Q4 (carried junk after a transition, FLAG-CS022-k) are unchanged from P1** and still unanswered. Paul says: these are fine.
- **Q8 — everything CS021's own gate never got to ask** (shield bounce off a rail, `ORBIT_ANG_VEL` 6 °/s, `ORBIT_FAST_MULT` 3.0, inbound passes with a full tow, whether `COMBO n/N` reads at a glance) is answerable for the first time now that the rings are threadable. Paul says: those are fine.

**✅ CS021's PLAYTEST GATE IS CLOSED — answered by Paul, and P5 has run on those answers.** **Outcome: ONE answer (Q1), seven unanswerable, and Q1 turned out to direct a FEATURE rather than a retune — so CS021 P5 retuned nothing and the geometry work opens CS022.** Q1 asks for the first ring 5 large-satellite diameters out and each subsequent ring 3 more, i.e. `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS` 180→460 and `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` 150→276; that puts the outermost satellite edge at **1,334 px against this world's 700 px wrap-clean budget**, so only ONE ring fits and it needs `min(WORLD_W, WORLD_H) ≥ 2708` — the per-level world-size feature Paul had already named for a later changeset. Paul's call, asked rather than assumed: **close CS021 now, geometry to CS022.** **CS022 MUST RE-RUN THIS GATE**, and Q2–Q8 below are still live questions, not answered ones — plus one that is new: at Q1's radii the shipped density curve yields **87** size-3 satellites rather than 40, so world size, ring geometry and the density curve need settling as ONE decision before the next playtest. The original gate text and Paul's own answers are kept verbatim below.

**⛔ CS021's PLAYTEST GATE (the CS021 P4→P5 round, kept verbatim for the record).** Eight questions, in `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS021.md`'s gate section. P5 must not run until Paul has played the P4 build through at least the first three orbit occurrences (levels 3, 6, 9) and ideally one past the 1.8 floor at level 24. The values P5 exists to carry are the density curve, `orbitAngVel`, `orbitFastMult`, and — if the floor feels wrong in the hands — the gap-mult curve. **Gate question 8 (the HUD readout) is answerable for the first time this session** — does `COMBO n/N` read at a glance mid-haul, and is the plain (no grace-window countdown) treatment enough, or does the gate want the countdown after all (spec §10's deferred detail — a FEATURE for a future changeset, not a P5 retune)? 
1. Fairness: The current orbits are too close to the dock -- they need to be further away. All of them are too tight. I would put the first orbit the equivalent of 5 large satellites out from the recycle dock at center. I would then put each subsequent orbit orbit the equivalent of 3 large satellites out from the orbit that is just inside it. This might mean the game level size needs to be larger, in order to accomodate all the orbits. That is okay. In a later changeset, we will ask for the ability to have different screen sizes in different levels, such as size 1 for just what fits on screen, size 4 which is 2 screens wide by 2 screens tall, size 9 which is 3 screens wide by 3 screens tall, and size 16, which is 4 screens wide by 4 screens tall. Keep that in mind if you have to increase level size to accomodate orbits. 
The rest of the playtest gate items 2 through 8 could not be tested practically, because of the orbits being too tight. We need to get the orbits further out, and then do another playtest gate iteration which must be answered here in STATUS.md before revisiting the items in Phase 5. Paul says: Fairness is fine.
**Answers go here before P5's session; P5 retunes from actual answers only, never an invented interpretation (CS020 P2 precedent).**

**Three things CS021 P1 adds to that gate, all first-pass and none of them specced — read these alongside the eight questions when the time comes:**

1. **THE SPAWN-SAFETY CLEARANCE METRIC (feeds gate Q6, "any wave start where the ship materialised inside or dangerously near a ring?").** The reroll requires the nearest satellite to be `minRequiredGap` (65 px) from the ship **centre to centre**, which is ~6 px of actual hull-to-satellite clearance at the floor and 66.3 px measured worst case over 120 real wave starts. The reasoning for centre-to-centre rather than surface-to-surface is in `spawnSafeOrbitLayout`'s comment and in the session log above — the short version is that surface-to-surface is unsatisfiable by construction for a ship sitting on a ring radius, so the bounded loop would exhaust on the normal path and "accept the last roll" could drop a satellite on the ship. **If a wave start ever feels like it began too close, this metric is the knob, and it is a one-expression change.**
2. **✅ CLOSED BY CS021 P1b — THE SHIP NOW BOUNCES OFF A RING (was: "the shield cannot push a ring").** Kept for the record and because two of its knobs are now gate material. The rail is still authoritative — `shieldDeflect` would shove a satellite and the next frame would put it straight back — so the shield no longer tries: `shieldBounce()` moves the SHIP instead, elastically, in the satellite's own frame. **What still wants judging at the gate: `SHIELD_BOUNCE_RESTITUTION` (1.0, "you get back exactly what you brought") and `SHIELD_BOUNCE_MIN` (120 px/s, the separation floor).** Ring 3 sweeping into a parked ship throws it at **302 px/s**; ring 1 at **120**. **Does a hard hit on a ring read as a satisfying bounce or as a trampoline that takes control away — especially inbound with a full tow?** Lower the restitution if it bounces too freely; lower the floor if a graze shoves too much.
3. **`ORBIT_ANG_VEL` 6 °/s AND `ORBIT_FAST_MULT` 3.0 (gate Q3, the fast ring).** One revolution per minute; ring 3 at 18 °/s ≈ 151 px/s tangential, between a size-2 and a size-1 debris. Nothing in the spec pins either. P3 exposes both as live sliders (`orbitAngVel` in °/s, `orbitFastMult` as a ×), so the gate answer is "the numbers I settled on while playing", not a yes/no.

**CS020 P1b — THE GATE BETWEEN P1b AND P2 (CLOSED, kept for the record).** Everything about the fix is headless-solid (187 assertions, mutation-tested against five deliberate breakages, and identical to a clean pass on every legitimate skirt) but nothing about how it FEELS has been seen in a browser. Four questions, priority order:

1. **FLAG-CS020-g — the window. This is the one value P2 exists to carry.** Skirt the dock at speed with a real load, several times, at different exit velocities. Does **4,000 ms** cover a normal overshoot-and-return? It ships at 4.0 s rather than the 2.0 first proposed because simulating `Ship.update`'s actual integrator says a 180° turn costs 0.75 s before thrust can even begin and a normal pilot exiting at 200–300 px/s with a real load is outside for 2.8–3.8 s — 2 s would have failed almost every real skirt. **Drag `Delivery one-effort window` in the hidden Debug Options panel** (new `DELIVERY` section, right after `CHAIN GUARD`) until it feels right and report the number. It is live: a change takes effect on the very next time you re-enter the ring, no restart. Since the hook reset is what bounds the counter, a generous window costs nothing — err long if in doubt.
2. **The one-effort rule in play (spec §5.4 — where this is STRICTER than the literal ask).** Skirt with 8, deliver 3, then hook one stray on the way back: the combo resets and the 5 originals deliver at 1…5 rather than 4…8, because you topped up mid-run and that makes it a new run. This is the load-bearing rule that keeps the counter capped, and it is the one case where the originals might have been expected to survive. **Does it ever read as unfair, or is it invisible?** If it reads badly, note it — but understand the fix is not "special-case it", it is a different bounding rule, and that is a CS021 conversation, not a P2 edit.
3. **The cap.** The combo ceiling is now exactly the payload capacity, by construction. Does that change how a full 24-load at level 12 feels — better (the ceiling is now something you can actually see yourself reach), or anticlimactic? Related: with no HUD readout of the combo anywhere (FLAG-CS020-i, deliberately CS021), is the ceiling even legible in play?
4. **P1 still good.** Does the parking behaviour still feel right after P1b, or did the one-effort rule make in-ring cleanup feel pointless? Headlessly it is unchanged — the 60-second level-1 park still pays exactly 37,500 with zero latches and no SMD, and the board still clears — but "unchanged number" and "still feels worth doing" are different questions.

**✅ CS020 P1's own gate CLOSED before this session** — FLAG-CS020-c, -d, -e and -f were all cleared in play and the parking exploit is dead. Its four questions are kept below for the record; nothing there is outstanding.

**CS020 P1 — THE GATE BETWEEN P1 AND P2 (CLOSED, kept for the record).** Everything about the fix is headless-solid (233 assertions, mutation-tested against five deliberate breakages, bit-identical to the pre-fix build on legitimate play) but nothing about how it FEELS has been seen in a browser. Four questions, priority order:

1. **FLAG-CS020-c — the repair residual, and this one comes with a measured number.** Incidentals still route through `addScore()`, which trips `REPAIR_MILESTONE` (10,000 points → 25 HP, or `REPAIR_FULL_BONUS` 2,500 points at full hull). Measured this session on the real path: a 60-second park fed ~10 canisters/s pays 30,000 flat and crosses **three** milestones; at the 20/s offload ceiling it would be one milestone every 10 seconds. **Park at the dock with a Magnet and a drained hull and see whether that reads as a healing station.** If it does, the fix is a new `DOCK_INCIDENTAL_SCORE` constant at ~10–15, a one-line change P2 can carry.
2. **FLAG-CS020-f — the straggler.** Tow a load in with a Magnet running. A piece dragged in during the final approach is tagged incidental and loses its multiplier, with no grace period. Does that read as unfair, or is it invisible? If unfair, the fix is a grace window and it is **not** a P2 change — it is CS021.
3. **FLAG-CS020-d/e — the feedback.** At high incidental throughput the dock can throw up to 20 `+50` floaters a second: legible, or confetti? And does the flat `AudioSys.deliver(1)` read as "recycled" or as "broken"?
4. **The feel of the fix overall.** Does parking still feel like a reasonable thing to do — clearing the board against the Kessler loop — or does it now feel pointless? Pointless would be a real loss; the mechanic was a nice find and the goal was to bound it, not kill it.

One thing the fix changes that is intended, not collateral, and worth watching for anyway: **Maxed Out is now effectively a level-12+ achievement** (a 24-piece TOW needs `payloadSlots` 24), and **Recycling Magnate / Salvage King are no longer farmable at the dock**.
